- **1.73.0** (2026-10-19): Phase CK-73.3 — **the private test notice, version 2: true about what is stored** (`decisions/2026-08-31-private-alpha-scope.md` 1.3.0 — §3 as amended by Steven 2026-10-19, §3.1 holding both texts word for word; the design session's finding of 2026-10-14 and the planning session's audit of 2026-10-19; **frontend `routes/Tos.tsx`, `lib/api.ts`, `routes/tos.test.tsx`; backend `api/auth.py`'s constant and `tests/test_auth.py` — no migration, head stays 0031, the verifier at 271 unchanged, neither lock touched, the worker and billing untouched**; **179 frontend tests** (177 + 2 — `routes/tos.test.tsx` 4 → 6), **602 backend tests** (600 + 2 — one test became three), with the moved pins listed below; two commits, one per root). **THE DEFECT**: `/tos` — the text the sign-in checkbox records agreement to, by number alone — said *"Photo upload doesn't exist yet, so no photos are stored"* from CK-36 (2026-09-09), when the first photograph was stored, until the design session found it on 2026-10-14; it also said *a development database* where photos live in a bucket and a plan at Stripe, and named none of: photos with their caption, tags and file name (CK-36–CK-40), RSVP answers and companions' names (CK-27/29), groups (CK-45), the addresses of people you invite (CK-25), a passkey (CK-10), what deletion leaves behind (anonymization — photos and answers stay under `ANONYMIZED_DISPLAY_NAME`, *a former member*; the omission that mattered most for consent), or test-mode plans (CK-73/74). **`routes/tos.test.tsx` PINNED the false sentence in place** — right at CK-26, and the reason it stayed: a pin on a claim about what is not built passes until the thing is built and goes on passing, so nine data-storing phases ran green past it and none opened the page. The project's recurring defect class, on its consent surface (the Gotcha below). **THE TEXT IS STEVEN'S, VERBATIM** (approved 2026-10-19; §3.1 — the one clause corrected after approval and reported to him: accepting an invitation does not clear the address, the pending row's week does): the opening, server-log, terms and closing paragraphs are version 1's byte for byte; *Everything is stored on test systems*, and a photo named as the thing to keep a copy of; the what-is-stored paragraph — identity, *the gatherings and groups you create or join*, answers *including the names of anyone you say you're bringing*, *the email addresses of people you invite, which are cleared after the invitation runs out a week later*, sign-in records, *a passkey if you add one*; a photographs paragraph — seen by the gathering with caption, tags and file name, *three copies of each photo at different sizes and deletes the file you uploaded*, *location and other camera details are removed*, your own deletable, *a photo sent to a bin is deleted for good 30 days later* (true only since `(gt)` switched the sweep on, 2026-10-19 — which is why that check was this phase's prerequisite), *Deleting your account removes your email address and name, but photos and answers you added stay in their gatherings under "a former member"*; a plans paragraph — *Stripe's test mode: no real payment is taken, and a real card won't work … never your card*. **Rendered and compared to §3.1 paragraph by paragraph: identical.** **Each sentence checked against the code before it was written**: `profile.py` (email NULL, `ANONYMIZED_DISPLAY_NAME`, every contribution kept), `invitations.py` (`INVITATION_TTL` seven days; `consumed_at` the only stamp at accept; reaped by `expires_at`; the accepted row carries a person id, never the address), `processing.py` (three layers, nothing but pixels to the encoder), `worker.py::_delete_original` after the `ready` commit, `destruction.py` (`REMOVED_BIN` 30 days), `config.py` (`rk_test_`). **THE VERSION**: `TOS_VERSION` → **2** on both sides, each with a comment saying what 1 and 2 are and that the published terms take **3** — the record's rule: an acceptance is recorded at account creation against the number alone (`api/auth.py::verify`), so one number must mean one text and a rewrite under 1 would make 1 mean two; **the validator stays `1 <= value <= TOS_VERSION`** — a browser still on the old bundle (the PWA keeps it until its update notice is taken) showed its user version 1's text, so 1 is the TRUE record for that sign-up, never a stale one; no migration, no backfill, every existing row stays 1 (every account is Steven's household, confirmed 2026-10-19; nobody is re-asked, and re-acceptance is queue item 0b's, for the published terms). **Tests**: `routes/tos.test.tsx` 4 → 6 — the *Photo upload doesn't exist* pin REPLACED BY ITS NEGATION over the whole rendered page (*no photos are stored* with it), the what-is-collected pin following the new wording, a second content test pinning three copies and the deleted original / location removed / your own deletable / 30 days / *a former member* / a week / test mode and never your card, `TOS_VERSION === 1` → `=== 2` with its comment rewritten (1, 2, 3; never renumber), the brand-source pin and the checkbox test unchanged — **the three content tests failed first against the old `Tos.tsx`** (swapped in from HEAD for one run, restored byte-identical); `tests/test_auth.py` — `_request_link` sends `auth_api.TOS_VERSION` (the helper models the current bundle), the records-the-version test rewritten without its monkeypatch as *version 2 records 2*, NEW *version 1 still records 1 — the stale-bundle case* and *3 is refused, the published terms are not published*, the unknown-version test given its reason. **The moved pins, each with its reason in the test**: the happy path's `tos[0].version == 1` → `== auth_api.TOS_VERSION` (the row records what the form sent); the `TOS_VERSION === 1` guard. **ONE CLAUSE FLAGGED, NOT CHANGED** (the kickoff's rule — no rewording): *"Delete them first if you'd rather they went"* is fully actionable for photos and only partly for answers — an RSVP can be changed to *Can't make it* and its companions removed, but no answer can be deleted (CK-27 shipped no delete endpoint, deliberately), so an anonymized person's answer row stays in the roster under *a former member*; **until the push no account has accepted version 2, so its text may still change under 2; after the first acceptance a change is version 3** — Steven's call before `(ja)`. Also noted: in console mode the invitation email, destination included, prints to the same server log the notice discloses for sign-in links. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) the baseline was 600 backend tests, not 585 — CK-73.2 had landed; (2) the record's Status line and §1 condition 4 still stated *version 1 / published terms = version 2* as current fact and are amended beside the BUILT banner (the living-description rule); (3) the happy-path pin above, which the kickoff's list did not name; (4) this file's Commands `npm run test` comment gains the notice's line. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Checks `(ja)`–`(jb)` are Steven's; `(jb)`'s throwaway account records 2 and every earlier row still reads 1. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-73.3-report.md`.
- **1.72.0** (2026-10-19): Phase CK-73.2 — **a downgrade waiting for renewal is stored and shown** (`decisions/2026-10-10-paid-tiers-on-stripe.md` 1.6.0 — §12 as amended 2026-10-19, §2's portal bullet, §7; the design session's finding of 2026-10-14: the plan screen could not say *switches to 100,000 photos on Nov 12*; **backend only: `models/billing.py`, migration 0031, `services/billing.py`, `api/billing.py`, `scripts/verify_schema.py` (261 → 271) and tests — plus `render.yaml`, the rider alone; nothing under `frontend/`, neither lock touched, the worker and `config.py` untouched**; **600 backend tests**, 15 new in `tests/test_billing.py` (ten functions, one parametrised over the six events), with the moved pins listed below; two commits, one per root). **WHAT HAPPENS AT STRIPE**: the Customer Portal downgrades at the end of the period (§7) with a SUBSCRIPTION SCHEDULE — the subscription's `schedule` names a `sub_sched_…` with two phases, the current price until the period ends and the lower price after — and CoveyKeep read the current item's price alone (§2), so the plan and the allowance stayed right until renewal, by design, and nothing knew a change was coming. **STEP 1, THE PROBE against the installed `stripe` 16.0.0, before anything was written**: `Subscription.schedule` is `str | SubscriptionSchedule | None` — an id string unexpanded, None with no schedule; `SubscriptionSchedule.phases[].items[].price` is `str | Price` — an id unexpanded; `SubscriptionRetrieveParams` carries `expand: List[str]`, and `retrieve(id, params={"expand": ["schedule.phases.items.price"]})` builds — four levels, Stripe's limit; `construct_from` with the nested schedule yields `SubscriptionSchedule`, `Phase`, `Item`, `Price` and `CurrentPhase` objects, every one subscript-readable and every one refusing `.get` (the CK-73 Gotcha); `SubscriptionSchedule.status` is `active | canceled | completed | not_started | released`, `.subscription` `str | Subscription | None`, `.released_subscription` `Optional[str]`. **THE SMALLEST FORM CHOSEN: ONE retrieve with that expansion** (`SUBSCRIPTION_EXPAND`, inside `_retrieve_subscription` — the one wrapper beneath the functions the routers call, so tests stub beneath the decision; pinned on the wrapper and on the SDK's own `expand` parameter), never a second fetch of the schedule by id. **MIGRATION 0031**: `subscriptions.scheduled_plan` (`billing_plan`, NULL) and `subscriptions.scheduled_plan_at` (timestamptz, NULL), no default, nothing backfilled (a migration cannot ask Stripe; the next event writes them, and on the deploy check (ix) makes one), under the CHECK **`ck_subscriptions_scheduled_plan_both_or_neither`** — `(scheduled_plan IS NULL) = (scheduled_plan_at IS NULL)`: a plan and an instant are one fact, and half of one is a row nothing wrote; the existing type is named and none of its labels (the CK-71 trap has nothing to bite on); invisible to the old instance under the pre-deploy ordering; the downgrade drops the CHECK and both columns; up/down/up pinned with a planted pair shown lost and the CHECK refused on a real row by name, both halves. **`sync_subscription` WRITES THEM** (`_scheduled_change`, everything through `_field`): when the schedule's status is `active` or `not_started`, the first phase whose `start_date` is at or after the current phase's `end_date` — or, with no current phase (the `not_started` shape), the item's period end — is the next phase; its one item's price names the plan by lookup key; that plan and that phase's start are written, **null both** for no schedule, no such phase, the same plan, or a schedule in any other status; **a next phase that cannot be read as a configured plan — an unknown lookup key, a price or a schedule that came back as an id — is ONE ERROR line naming the event and subscription ids (the tenth line under the CK-73.1 enumeration), both columns null, and the rest of the upsert written as usual**: it never blocks the current plan. **THE ALLOWANCE NEVER READS THEM** — pinned behaviourally (300,000 and `holds_paid_plan` True with a 100k change scheduled; 100,000 once the schedule completes and the item moves) and structurally (a token scan of `keeping.py` and `api/media.py` for `scheduled`; the body's figure from `keeping.PLAN_PHOTOGRAPHS`, never a column; `next_allowance` and `scheduled_allowance` asserted absent by the verifier). **`GET /billing` CARRIES `scheduled_change`** — `{"plan", "allowance", "at"}` or null; **null whenever `ends_at` is set** (a plan that ends has no next plan, and Stripe releases the schedule on cancel anyway — CK-73.1's finding; should both arrive together the ending wins on the surface and the row still holds what Stripe holds, pinned in both cancellation shapes); every other key unchanged. **THE WEBHOOK LEARNS THE SIX SCHEDULE EVENTS** — `subscription_schedule.created` / `.updated` / `.released` / `.canceled` / `.completed` / `.aborted` (`SCHEDULE_EVENT_TYPES`; `HANDLED_EVENT_TYPES` exactly ten) — because a schedule can change without any field on the subscription itself changing, so `customer.subscription.updated` alone can miss it: `subscription_id_of` reads a schedule event's `subscription`, else `released_subscription`, else None — a schedule naming neither is a 200 with nothing done and no "handled" line; each handled one is the same nudge (fetch by id, upsert, the same 503 on a fetch failure, the same 200 and ERROR on `resource_missing`, one INFO line with the type and id); **not `.expiring`**, a seven-day reminder nothing reads, pinned unhandled. **No permission change**: Stripe maps the schedule endpoints to `subscription_read`, which the key holds at Write. **THE VERIFIER → 0031, 271** (ten new, not eight — below): both columns nullable (the column loop), `scheduled_plan` on `billing_plan` by `udt_name` and `scheduled_plan_at` timestamptz, no server default on either (`.first()`), the CHECK by name, the pair invariant as data (guarded on the columns), and `next_allowance` / `scheduled_allowance` absent — negative-tested by a temporary downgrade in the 0031 round trip (262 / 9 at 0030; the 0030 round trip now reads 259 / 12 at 0029, 0031's nine failing beside cancel_at's three) and by a plant with the CHECK dropped and recreated (269 / 2: the shape line and the data line). **THE RIDER RAN — prerequisite 3 was met**: `WORKING-ON-NOW.md` 1.176.0 records `(gt)` PASSED on 2026-10-19 (capture 19:55 UTC, switch 20:17 UTC, startup `sweep=on`, two rows destroyed as predicted), so `render.yaml`'s `SWEEP_ENABLED` is `"true"` with its comment rewritten — the file says what the worker does, and a Blueprint sync can no longer put the dashboard back to off; **`WorkerSettings.sweep_enabled`'s default is untouched** (a local worker and the suite stay dark; the field-list pin at seven and its `default is False` unedited; the one comment in `test_worker.py` that said the slot was declared `"false"` restated). **The moved pins, each with its reason in the test**: `GET /billing`'s body key set (gains `scheduled_change` — three exact-dict pins); the fixtures — `_plant_subscription` gains the two columns and `_subscription_object` emits `schedule` (null, an id, or the expanded object) as Stripe always sends it; the 0030 round trip's downgraded tally (257 / 4 → 259 / 12 — a downgrade to 0029 passes through 0031); `test_photo_count.py`'s `HEAD` → 0031 with its comment; the tallies in `test_co_hosts.py`, `test_destruction.py`, `test_failure_reason.py` and `test_billing.py` 261/260/259/258 → 271/270/269/268, their comments extended; every existing `caplog` test unedited and green. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) the verifier adds TEN lines, not eight — two never-stored columns asserted absent in CK-73's own discipline, so every tally moved by ten; (2) the boundary for a schedule with no current phase (`not_started`) is the item's period end — the kickoff's rule names the current phase's end alone, which such a schedule lacks; (3) an unexpanded schedule or price is folded into the same ERROR line as an unknown key rather than silently null — one new log line, not two, and a broken expansion stays visible; (4) `reference/operations/deploy.md`'s *Verifying a deploy* step 5 still said the verifier reads 258 / 0 — stale since CK-73.1 — and is corrected to the head's total (touched beyond the rider); (5) the record's DATA-HANDLING clause, this file's Commands comment and its Deploys worker bullet were touched beyond the kickoff's list because each stated the old shape as current (the living-description rule). Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Checks (iw)–(iz) are Steven's, sandbox and test cards only; **(iz) is DATED, on or after 2026-11-12** — the renewal-time switch the record has called unverified since CK-73. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-73.2-report.md`.
- **1.71.0** (2026-10-12): Phase CK-73.1 — **the API's own log lines reach the deploy, and a portal cancellation is recognised** (`decisions/2026-10-10-paid-tiers-on-stripe.md` 1.4.0 — §7 and §11 as amended; the two defects CK-73's deploy checks (iq) and (ir) found on 2026-10-11/12; **backend only: a new `app/logging_setup.py`, `main.py`, `models/billing.py`, migration 0030, `services/billing.py`, `api/billing.py`, `scripts/verify_schema.py` (258 → 261) and tests — nothing under `frontend/`, neither lock touched, the worker untouched**; **585 backend tests**, 6 new in `tests/test_billing.py`, with the moved pins listed below; two commits, one per root). **DEFECT 1 — THE API WROTE NONE OF ITS OWN INFO LINES**: check (iq) looked for the webhook's *one INFO line per handled event* in Render's log and found uvicorn's access lines alone. The web process never configured logging — `app/worker.py::main` calls `basicConfig(level=INFO, stream=stdout)` for the worker, and nothing in the API does — so `covey-keep.billing`'s INFO records reached a root logger at Python's default WARNING with no handler and were dropped; its WARNINGs surfaced only through the last-resort handler. **Every INFO line the API was ever meant to write had been silent**, CK-73's the first anyone looked for. **THE FIX, decided by the planning session**: `app/logging_setup.py::configure_logging`, called once at `app.main`'s import — the **`covey-keep` namespace** at INFO with ONE marked handler to stdout in the worker's format (`LOG_FORMAT`, pinned equal to the worker's source literal), idempotent (a second call, a reimport, uvicorn's reloader: the mark is found and nothing is added — pinned in-process and in a fresh interpreter through `importlib.reload`); **NOT the root** (only our lines become visible, never a library's INFO line by accident — the `stripe` WARNING and `botocore` pins stand, pinned); **`propagate` stays True** (the suite's `caplog` captures at the root, and CK-73's never-log pins would pass VACUOUSLY on a namespace that stopped propagating; in production the root has no handler — pinned in a fresh interpreter: WARNING and zero handlers before and after `import app.main`, the line on STDOUT alone); the worker untouched and never loading the module (pinned beside the CK-59 graph pin). **THE ENUMERATION** (STEP 1, the report): every INFO-or-above call under `app/` the web import graph reaches — `app.services.ingest` and `app.worker` are not in it — is on `covey-keep.billing`: eight at CK-73 and one new, carrying event ids, subscription ids, plan names and a Stripe status word; none can carry an email, a URL, a key, a payload, a filename, a caption or a tag. **DEFECT 2 — A PORTAL CANCELLATION WAS NOT RECOGNISED**: check (ir) cancelled in the portal, which read *Cancels Nov 12* with the subscription `active`, and `GET /billing` still said `cancel_at_period_end: false` — **under `2026-09-30.endive` the portal cancels by setting `cancel_at` to the period-end instant and leaving the boolean false**, and `sync_subscription` read only the boolean; the allowance and status were right throughout, the "this plan is ending" fact lost. (Cancelling also released the scheduled downgrade — the `schedule` went null — harmless and recorded.) **THE FIX**: **migration 0030** — `subscriptions.cancel_at` timestamptz NULL, no default, **nothing backfilled** (a migration cannot ask Stripe; the next event writes it, and on the deploy check (iv) does), invisible to the old instance under the pre-deploy ordering, the downgrade dropping it; `sync_subscription` writes it through `_field` as an instant from Stripe's epoch seconds, null written like any value ("Don't cancel"); **`GET /billing` carries `ends_at` in place of `cancel_at_period_end`** — `_ends_at`: `cancel_at` when set, else `current_period_end` when the boolean is true, else null — derived from the two facts stored verbatim and never stored itself, so the one reader (CK-74's plan screen) gets the answer rather than two fields to combine; the allowance reads neither (a plan with an end date is paid until Stripe says it is not — pinned). **THE RIDER, the record's own rule applied to the fetch**: a `resource_missing` from `fetch_subscription` (which now answers None, `close_customer`'s shape) is a finding a retry cannot fix — a **200**, nothing written, one ERROR line naming the event and subscription ids and no "handled" line, never the 503 that would have Stripe retry for days; a permission refusal and the network down are still the 503 (check (is) showed the deletion path does not reach it; the gap was the fetch). **THE VERIFIER → 0030, 261**: `cancel_at` nullable (the column loop), `timestamp with time zone` and no server default (`.first()`), negative-tested by a temporary downgrade in the 0030 round-trip test — the verifier failing on exactly four lines, the head and the three. **The moved pins**: `GET /billing`'s body key set (`cancel_at_period_end` → `ends_at`, with its reason in the test); `test_photo_count.py`'s `HEAD` → 0030 with its comment; the tallies in `test_co_hosts.py`, `test_destruction.py`, `test_failure_reason.py` and `test_billing.py` 258/257/256/255 → 261/260/259/258, their comments extended; every existing `caplog` test unedited and green. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) prerequisite 1 was NOT met — the Hub was clean but one commit ahead of its remote (the planning session's own commit, which wrote this kickoff); the push is Steven's, the CK-66/CK-68 precedent applied, the breach heads the report; (2) the record was touched beyond the two banners the kickoff named — a third, short banner before §9 (its CK-73 banner said *no column beyond §9's list*, false once 0030 adds one) and one clause in DATA-HANDLING (*the cancel flag* — now the cancellation date too), the living-description rule; (3) the enumeration counts nine lines, not eight — the rider's ERROR is the ninth and is this phase's own. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Checks (it)–(iv) are Steven's, sandbox and test cards only; (iv) re-syncs the one subscription so its row gains its `cancel_at`. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-73.1-report.md`.
- **1.70.0** (2026-10-11): Phase CK-73 — **paid tiers, the backend: two photo plans on Stripe, and the allowance read from a local row** (`decisions/2026-10-10-paid-tiers-on-stripe.md` 1.2.0 — the backend half BUILT; the frontend is CK-74; **backend only: `requirements.in` + both locks regenerated, `config.py`, `models/enums.py`, a new `models/billing.py`, migration 0029, `services/keeping.py`, a new `services/billing.py`, a new `api/billing.py` (the eleventh router), `api/profile.py`, `main.py`, `render.yaml`, `.env.example`, `scripts/verify_schema.py` (219 → 258) and tests; NOTHING under `frontend/`** (177 unchanged); **579 backend tests**, 43 new in `tests/test_billing.py`, with the head and tally pins moved (listed below); two commits, one per root). **THE DEPENDENCY, ON THE LOCKED TREE**: `stripe==16.0.0` (uploaded 2026-10-01, ten days before this phase; API version **`2026-09-30.endive`**, the SDK's own constant, which the webhook endpoint is registered against — it equals the account default the record §2 records, and the code relies on the constant, not the account's setting) with the fourth deliberate-exception comment in `requirements.in`; both locks regenerated with the documented commands, **42 → 46 packages and NO already-locked version moved** — proved by a parse of both locks before and after: the four new are `stripe` 16.0.0, `requests` 2.34.2 (2026-05-14), `charset-normalizer` 3.5.2 (2026-09-30) and `certifi` 2026.7.22 (2026-07-22, already in the dev lock at that version — a scratch constraints file held it there, and the unconstrained recompile INTO THE SEEDED OUTPUT reproduced the lock byte-identical outside one `# via` line, the CK-72 proof; a recompile into a FRESH path resolves afresh and moved pydantic, which is why the seeded form is the one that counts); the venv rebuilt from the two locks (`pip check` clean), `test_requirements_lock.py` green unedited. **THE ALLOWANCE IS DERIVED, NEVER STORED**: migration 0029 creates `billing_provider` (`stripe`) and `billing_plan` (`photos_100k`, `photos_300k`) by `CREATE TYPE`, `billing_customers` (`account_id` PK → accounts, `provider`, `provider_customer_id`, UNIQUE (provider, provider_customer_id)) and `subscriptions` (`id`, `account_id` indexed → accounts, `provider`, `provider_subscription_id` UNIQUE with provider, `plan`, **`status` TEXT — Stripe's value verbatim, never an enum** — `current_period_end` NULL, `cancel_at_period_end`, `created_at`, `updated_at` nullable) with **the partial unique `uq_subscriptions_account_id_paid` over `status IN ('active', 'past_due')`** — the database's backstop for one paid subscription per account, its predicate pinned to agree with `PAID_STATUSES` — no FK delete rule, no allowance column, no is-paid boolean (the verifier asserts the absence). `services/keeping.py`: `PAID_STATUSES = {active, past_due}` (a card Stripe is still retrying keeps the plan) and `PLAN_PHOTOGRAPHS` beside `FREE_TIER_PHOTOGRAPHS`; `paid_subscription` the ONE definition of paid, `holds_paid_plan` beside it for the keeping flows, and `account_quota` reading the row — its signature unchanged, so `api/media.py::_enforce_limits` has no diff; **the quota path never imports stripe** (pinned three ways: a token scan of `keeping.py` and `api/media.py`, `services/billing.py` the one importer under `app/`, and `'stripe' not in sys.modules` after `import app.worker` in a subprocess, with `import app.main` loading it as the positive). **THE SEAM** (`services/billing.py`, the one module that imports `stripe`): a lazily built `StripeClient` from the restricted key with `max_network_retries=2` and a ten-second `RequestsClient` timeout (the SDK's default is eighty, and a request handler waits on it), every call through `asyncio.to_thread`; six one-call wrappers that tests stub BENEATH the decisions — `ensure_customer` (the row read without the lock first, then the account row locked `FOR UPDATE` to create, with an idempotency key derived from the account id beneath it: two concurrent clicks make ONE customer, pinned with `asyncio.gather`), `price_for` by lookup key (an unpriced key a 503 and an ERROR naming the plan), `checkout_url` (`mode=subscription`, one line item, `client_reference_id` and `subscription_data.metadata.account_id`, the two `/plan` return URLs, no promotion codes, no automatic tax — pinned as the exact dict), `portal_url`, `fetch_subscription`, and **`close_customer`, which deletes the customer ALONE** — the SDK's own `CustomerService.delete` docstring: *"Permanently deletes a customer. It cannot be undone. Also immediately cancels any active subscriptions on the customer."* — with `resource_missing` a success; **`sync_subscription`** keyed on (provider, provider_subscription_id), the account through `billing_customers` and never metadata (an unknown customer dropped at INFO with ids), the plan through the price's lookup key (an unknown key an ERROR naming the event id, nothing written), the status verbatim with a WARNING outside the six the product can produce, **`current_period_end` read from the subscription ITEM** (`items.data[].current_period_end`, where API versions since 2025-03-31 put it — the pinned SDK declares it on `SubscriptionItem` and not on `Subscription`, pinned against an object built with the SDK's own `construct_from`), a second paid subscription for one account an ERROR naming both ids and nothing written (the IntegrityError race caught the same way); the `stripe` logger pinned at **WARNING** at import (its INFO lines carry request URLs, its DEBUG lines bodies). **THE ROUTER** (`api/billing.py`): `GET /billing` (plan or null, status, period end, cancel flag, the allowance through `account_quota` — the number the gate enforces — `next` checkout | portal, never a Stripe id), `POST /billing/checkout` (`{"plan"}`; an unknown plan FastAPI's 422 at `["body","plan"]` with nothing called; **409 `already_subscribed`** for `active` and `past_due` alike, a `canceled` row not refusing; the customer committed before the next Stripe call so the row lock is released), `POST /billing/portal` (404 "No such plan." with no customer), `POST /billing/stripe/webhook` (unauthenticated; the raw body verified by the SDK — a missing or bad signature, or a signed body that is not JSON, a 400 with nothing done and one WARNING line; the four handled types fetch by id then upsert, 200; every other type 200 and nothing; a fetch failure 503 so Stripe retries; one INFO line per handled event — type and id — pinned with the root at DEBUG against a payload carrying an email, a name and an address). **A Checkout or portal URL appears in the body and in no log record** (pinned with the root at DEBUG). **THE DELETION LEG** (`api/profile.py`, the contract's first outbound call): before the transaction, an account with a customer has it closed at Stripe — proven called BEFORE the anonymization by a stub that reads the person's row at the instant of the call; any error but "already gone" is a **503 `billing_unavailable`** with nothing anonymized, every row standing and the session live; the transaction then deletes the account's `subscriptions` and `billing_customers` rows; no customer, no call. **CONFIG**: four required fields on `Settings` — `stripe_api_key` boot-checked to start `rk_test_` (a full-access `sk_` key, a live `rk_live_` key, a publishable key and garbage each refused naming the field and the shape and never the value), `stripe_webhook_secret` to start `whsec_`, the two lookup keys plain — `WorkerSettings` unchanged at seven with no Stripe field; four `sync: false` slots on `covey-keep-api` alone in `render.yaml`; the four in `conftest.py` and `.env.example`; the local shell placeholder block gains them. **THE VERIFIER → 0029, 258**: 39 new — both tables' shapes, the three absent columns on each, `status` as text, the two types' labels, the two uniques, the partial unique by name WITH its predicate, the account index, both FKs with no delete rule, and four invariants (every subscription's account has a customer row of the same provider; no billing row on an anonymized account, both tables; no account with two paid subscriptions) — negative-tested as a subprocess on three plants (257/1, 256/2, and 255/3 with the partial index DROPPED for the plant and recreated after), each plant confirmed by its own SELECT; the 0029 round trip pinned with a row planted at head shown lost. **The moved pins**: `test_photo_count.py`'s `HEAD` → 0029 with its comment; the tallies in `test_co_hosts.py`, `test_destruction.py` and `test_failure_reason.py` 219/218 → 258/257, their comments extended. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) **a `StripeObject` is NOT a dict in this SDK — `.get` and `.items` raise** — so the seam reads fields by subscript through one helper, `_field` (the Gotcha below), found by the first probe against the installed SDK before any test ran; (2) the import-graph pin scans CODE tokens (comments and docstrings stripped, the CK-70 idiom) rather than the whole file, because the quota module's prose says "never calls Stripe" on purpose; (3) the `stripe` logger is held at WARNING, stricter than the kickoff's "above DEBUG", because its INFO lines carry request URLs and a URL is on the never-log list; not `not_ready`, which means "wait"), then ONE guarded `DELETE … WHERE id AND status = 'failed' AND uploader_person_id = <the caller>`, a rowcount of 0 the 404; the tags go by the CASCADE; no store call (the router still imports neither `ingest` nor a storage delete — pinned); no count moves (`photo_count`, `total_bytes`, the keeper's usage — pinned); nothing logged; no `dismissed_at`, no bin, no window, no reap — a failed row holds nothing but the person's own filename and tags, and the operator's record is the worker's dead-letter WARNING line. **THE BACKFILL**, the one-time exception to "never derived": STEP 0 enumerated every text a `failed` row could have carried since CK-35 from `ingest.py`'s history — four texts, every prefix unmoved across the thirteen commits that touched the file (the table is in the report) — and 0028 classifies by prefix, `too_large` before the general `unreadable`, reporting the count per reason and the count LEFT NULL on alembic's INFO line (zero expected; measured on PostgreSQL 16.15, a label added by `ADD VALUE` is unsafe in its transaction even when the type was created in it, which makes 0025's "a fresh test database is the one exemption" the wrong way round; - **1.67.0** (2026-09-30): Phase CK-70 — **a JPEG that carries a second image is still a photograph: decode MPO's primary frame** (found at CK-69's checks — `IMG_1721.jpeg`, row `9ad10eeb-…`, 1,807,499 bytes, declared `image/jpeg`, dead-lettered *could not process the upload as a photograph: MPO is not a supported photograph format (permanent)*; **backend only: `services/processing.py`, two comments in `api/media.py`, `tests/fixtures/media/` and tests — NO migration, head stays 0027, the verifier at 211 unchanged, NO frontend change, NO change to the intent allowlist or any refusal copy**; **515 backend tests**, 3 new — `test_processing.py` 2 (the MPO fixture through the parametrised GPS test, and the MPO test of its own), `test_worker.py` 1 — with **one pin changed deliberately**, below; two commits, one per root). **THE DEFECT**: some JPEGs carry more than one image — the Multi-Picture Format (MPF, CIPA DC-007), which phones write to embed an HDR gain map or a portrait depth map after the photograph — and **Pillow names such a file `MPO`, not `JPEG`**; `DECODABLE_FORMATS` was `{JPEG, PNG, WEBP, HEIF}`, so the intent's `image/jpeg` admitted it and the decoder refused it: the allowlist and the decoder disagreed, which CK-36's pin exists to prevent and could not see, because it pinned format NAMES rather than what a phone writes under an admitted type. Deterministic, not transient (a later upload that worked was a different copy; the deployed decoder unchanged since 2026-09-16). **STEP 1, reproduced and enumerated before anything changed**: a generated two-image MPO drew the exact deployed message from the unmodified `render_layers`; then every format name `Image.open` can report for bytes a phone would label with one of the five admitted types was enumerated from the INSTALLED Pillow 12.3.0 and pillow-heif 1.7.0 — 26 samples: baseline, progressive, EXIF-bearing, ICC-bearing and 4:4:4 JPEGs, two- and three-image MPOs, an Android Ultra HDR JPEG, PNG and APNG, lossy, lossless, EXIF-bearing and animated WebP, pillow-heif's HEIF, the committed HEIC fixture and the same container under every HEIF major brand — with every registered opener's `accept` listed per sample: `image/jpeg` → `JPEG` or `MPO`; `image/png` → `PNG`; `image/webp` → `WEBP`; `image/heic`/`heif` → `HEIF`; nothing else, so nothing else was admitted. **THE FIX**: `DECODABLE_FORMATS` gains `MPO` and its comment is rewritten to list the real names per admitted type (the MPF; the primary frame as the photograph; **only frame 0 ever decoded — nothing seeks**; every other image dropped by the rule that drops every other embedded byte; an HDR gain map therefore not applied, the layers the standard-range base image; the pixel guard reading frame 0's dimensions); `api/media.py`'s allowlist comment and module docstring gain the missing clause — **one content type can arrive as more than one real format name**. **THE FIXTURE, the CK-36 discipline**: `gps-mpo.jpg` — `gps-oriented.jpg`'s primary frame exactly (same pixels, same GPS IFD and orientation 6, same quality) with a small solid-GREEN second image appended through Pillow's MPF writer; green rather than the kickoff's suggested magenta, because the primary's pixels are red and blue, whose every blend and compression artefact keeps green near zero (measured: the layers' highest green channel is 28 of 255), so one green pixel in a layer is the second image showing through; regenerated by `make_fixtures.py` with the other three fixtures byte-identical. **The pins**: the fixture first shown to be what it claims (MPO, two frames, GPS and orientation 6 in the primary, the second frame green); three layers, each single-frame and the layer's own format, no MPF marker, GPS absent both ways, upright pixel by pixel, not one green pixel; **and BYTE-IDENTICAL to the JPEG fixture's layers** — which is how `draft`, `load` and `exif_transpose` treating an MPO exactly as a JPEG (`MpoImageFile` subclasses `JpegImageFile`) is shown rather than asserted; the module's code never names `.seek(` (comment lines excluded); and the worker publishes the MPO fixture end to end — `ready`, attempts 1, three derivative rows, `live` in the open fixture gathering, every stored layer single-frame, MPF-free, GPS-free and upright. **The changed pin, with its reason in the test**: `test_the_decodable_formats_are_exactly_the_allowlists_real_counterpart` → the five names, its decode loop now one sample per name, each first shown to carry that name (a generated MPO among them; the HEIF sample the committed fixture). Unchanged and passing: the GIF/BMP/TIFF refusal, the pixel guard, the truncated-JPEG and renamed-`.mov` refusals. **Recorded, not acted on**: Pillow 12.3.0 reports an Android Ultra HDR JPEG (MPF plus an `hdrgm` XMP) as plain `JPEG`, base image only — a later Pillow naming it `MPO` lands in the same set with the same outcome; and Pillow's native AVIF plugin also accepts a `mif1`/`msf1`-branded HEIF, but its factory raises `SyntaxError` on one, so `Image.open` moves on to pillow-heif whatever the registration order (in the worker's process `HEIF` is registered first regardless — `processing.py` registers it at import, before Pillow's own plugins). **Not done, by the kickoff**: no `failed` row revived — `9ad10eeb-…` stays `failed`, its original deleted at the dead-letter; the person uploads again. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) green, not magenta, above; (2) this file was touched beyond the kickoff's three items — the directory map's `tests/` and `fixtures/media/` entries describe the fixture set and the two test files and would otherwise state three fixtures (the CK-62/CK-65 precedent: living descriptions must not state the old behaviour); (3) `api/media.py`'s module docstring was amended beside the comment the kickoff named — two claims about one rule must agree. a co-host's seat is `isHost={false} organises={true}`). **THE REMOVED VIEW LISTS GATHERING-BIN ROWS ONLY, AND EVERY ROW IN IT IS TREATED AS ONE, WHOEVER UPLOADED IT**: an organiser's own photograph that another organiser removed renders the gathering-bin line and the gathering-bin controls there — never "In your bin", never the personal controls (`mediaStateMessage`'s `gatheringBin` flag; the loaders renamed for the set they check, `_gathering_for_organiser` / `_occurrence_for_organiser` (delegable) and `_gathering_for_host` (reserved), and **every call site moved to one or the other on purpose** (no bare "admin" names a gathering role anywhere in the backend). **THE CLASSIFICATION, verified against the code** (one line per check in `reference/backend/api-reference.md`'s Co-hosts router): DELEGABLE — the gathering's PATCH (every field but the review switch), occurrences (add, move, remove — and the RSVP-count disclosure its 409 carries), invitations (create, list, revoke — the revoke had inlined its own host comparison, not a loader), the full RSVP list in every mode, taking a published photograph down, reading the gathering's bin and restoring from it, the co-host list, stepping down; RESERVED — `pending` visibility, the queue, publish, decline, the batch, the review switch (**the PATCH is field-level**: a co-host sending `requires_approval_override` draws a 422 on that field and the whole request is refused, nothing else applied), destroying someone else's photograph (emptying the gathering's bin included), adding or removing co-hosts; UNCHANGED — the upload (the interim read audience), a person's own photographs, the read audience itself (a co-host is in it by construction, and the relation grants no read), the keeper's audience. **`api/media.py`**: `_moderates` IS the SQL `may_administer` — CK-66's one named place, widened as its docstring promised — so a co-host reads the gathering's bin (`?removed=true`, `/url`) and takes a published photograph down (the co-host as remover); **`_may_remove` split** into `_may_send_to_bin` (host, co-host, uploader) and `_may_destroy` (host, uploader — never a co-host), so `remove` widened and `destroy` did not; **a co-host's restore passes through the gate** — `restore_media`'s third path, the uploader's destination with the gathering-bin restorer condition, one guarded UPDATE per path, `_gate_destination` extracted so the two non-host paths share one reading of the ladder (gated → `pending`, back to the host and out of the co-host's own sight; open → `live` with a NULL publisher on a never-published row); the local `_is_host` deleted — one definition, imported. **THE THREE ENDPOINTS** (`api/co_hosts.py`, the tenth router): `POST /gatherings/{id}/co-hosts` — the host alone, the subject an accepted invitee named by **the accepted invitation's id, the one handle the host already reads** in `GET …/invitations` (never an email, a person id or an account id), 422s with stable codes `not_invited` / `is_host` / `already_co_host` / `account_deleted` (the last a check the planning list did not name: deletion ends co-hosting and an add must not undo it), everyone else the gathering 404; `GET /gatherings/{id}/co-hosts` — the organisers, display names and the handle, `is_self`; `DELETE /gatherings/{id}/co-hosts/{invitation_id}` — the host removes anyone (idempotent), a co-host removes exactly themselves, a co-host naming another is hidden (the co-host 404). **`caller_role`** — `host` | `co_host` | `null` — on the create, detail, list (an `EXISTS` column in the one statement; the CK-20 pin passes unedited) and patch bodies; never the co-host list itself. **The deletion leg** (`services/keeping.py::lapse_kept_statuses`, a fourth write): the account's co-host rows DELETED in the transaction that relinquishes host — a row is the role, there is no "needs a co-host" state — and the rows it added stay as provenance. **A co-host row on a hostless gathering still organises** (co-hosts §7's first item, provisionally), nobody is host there, and the record's second item stays open (the answer built is no). **Recorded, not fixed**: a keeper who holds no accepted invitation has no handle the host can read and cannot be made a co-host through this surface (every keeper on the deploy is the host); the list rule's pre-existing gap — kept-or-invited since CK-49b, so a host who is not the keeper does not see their own gathering in their list (the sponsorship shape, psql-only on the deploy). **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) **the prerequisite was NOT met** — the Hub's `WORKING-ON-NOW.md` was dirty at 1.149.0 (the planning session's own entry) when the phase began; (3) this file was touched beyond "bump + changelog" — the CK-54 and CK-66 Deploys bullets and the directory-map entries stated the old audience as current behaviour (the CK-62/CK-65/CK-66 precedent). - **1.62.0** (2026-09-28): Phase CK-66 — **the gathering's bin: the host reads it, restores from it, and empties it one photograph at a time** (`decisions/2026-09-27-two-bins.md` §1 and §4's host half BUILT; **backend only: `api/media.py` and tests — NO migration, head stays 0026, the verifier at 197 unchanged; NO frontend change — the surface is CK-67**; **493 backend tests**, 14 new in `tests/test_gathering_bin.py`, **five existing expectations changed deliberately, each with its reason in the test**; two commits, one per root). **THE WIDENING IS ONE SEAT**: `_visible_media`'s `removed` branch gains branch (b) — the HOST, where the RECORDED remover is someone other than the uploader (a moderation removal or a decline), within the same `REMOVED_BIN` window — behind **`_moderates`**, ONE named SQL expression, so co-hosts (read and restore, never empty — co-hosts §4 as amended by two-bins) widen exactly one place; `destroy` never reads it (it stays behind `_may_remove`), so widening `_moderates` can never widen who destroys. The bin's SHAPE is **`_in_gathering_bin`**: `removed`, remover NOT NULL, remover **`IS DISTINCT FROM`** uploader — never `!=`, because a guest upload's NULL uploader makes `!=` NULL and would silently hide the one removed row no uploader exists to re-upload (pinned at the criterion AND the guarded UPDATE, through a planted guest row). **THE VIEW**: `?removed=true` on the media list — gathering-bin rows only, composing with `q`; **no host test is written on the flag at all** — the criterion composed with visibility IS the host test — so a non-host reads a 200 and an EMPTY list, never a refusal that confirms the bin exists; **the DEFAULT list excludes gathering-bin rows** in the same breath (the bin is a view, never a mixture) while the uploader's personal-bin rows stay in their default list (CK-64's surface depends on it); the two flags together draw a 422 at `["query","removed"]`. **HOST RESTORE GOES `live`, WHATEVER THE GATE SAYS** (two-bins §4 — only a restore by someone other than the host passes back through the gate; the host putting it back IS the host approving it): the publication pair is kept WHOLE where `published_at` is set — "keep it if set" read at the PAIR level, so a rule-published row keeps its NULL publisher (a recorded fact, not a blank to fill; the kickoff's per-column letter would have written the host's id over it, and its own "stamps kept" test spec decided the reading — recorded) — and a declined never-published row is stamped at restore time with the HOST's person id: a person published it, not the rule. The uploader's restore is byte-identical to CK-63's (its gated-and-open tests pass unedited); ONE guarded UPDATE either way, the same lost-race 409, no count moves, and the verifier's four stamp invariants re-run as SQL after every act (a new `_stamp_invariants_hold` beside `test_destruction`'s `_invariants_hold`). **NULL-remover rows stay hidden from everyone** — the two 2026-09-14 declines untouched; the uploader of a host-removed row still draws the byte-identical 404 on `/url`, `/restore` and `/destroy`; the keeper, invitees and strangers gain nothing; **the sweep is untouched**. **The five changed expectations, each stating its reason**: test_removal_attribution's uploader-sees test (the host's `/url` on the two gathering-bin rows, 404 → 200; - **1.59.0** (2026-09-28): Phase CK-64.1 — **the bin never promises more days than it has** (the one wording defect CK-64's deploy checks found; **frontend only: `lib/media.ts` and `lib/media-bin.test.ts` — NO backend change, no migration, head stays 0026, the verifier at 197 unchanged**; **134 frontend tests**, 2 new, **no existing test edited**; build + lint clean; two commits, one per root). **THE DEFECT**: check `(he)` passed on data, but a photograph binned seconds earlier read *“you can put it back for 31 more days”* against a 30-day window. `binDaysLeft` counts from `removed_at`, stamped by the SERVER's clock, to `now`, read from the DEVICE's, and rounds up. When the device trails the server by even a second, a fresh removal has the whole window plus that second left, and `Math.ceil` made it 31. CK-64's tests read both instants from one clock, so none could see it. **Not a timezone bug**: `removed_at` is `timestamptz`, serialised with its offset. **THE FIX IS ONE CLAMP**: the counting path returns `Math.min(REMOVED_BIN_DAYS, Math.ceil(remaining / DAY_MS))`. Rounding up stays below the cap (29.9 days left still reads 30), the `< DAY_MS → 0` and `null` paths are unchanged, and the copy, the rounding direction and every other function are untouched. The comment above it names the two clocks and the cap. It also says why a device clock AHEAD of the server's is the acceptable direction: it can read one day short near a day's turn and at the end, which under-promises. Nothing corrects for skew, and no server time is sent to do it; the server's audience rule decides when the way back closes. One residue is recorded in the comment rather than fixed: below the cap, a trailing clock delays each day's tick by the skew, as a page left open delays it anyway. Only at the top could it name a number the window never holds. **Tests** (`lib/media-bin.test.ts`, appended after the existing bin-line test, which is untouched): `now` one second, one hour and one day BEFORE `removed_at` reads 30, and 29.9 days left reads 30 (the cap takes nothing from ceil); the bin's line from a clock one second and one hour behind reads *“…for 30 more days.”* from both seats. **Both failed first against the unfixed code, reading 31**, before the clamp was written. The existing cases (30 at removal, 28 at 2.5 days, 1, 0, null) pass untouched. `reference/frontend/components.md` 1.21.0 → 1.21.1 adds one sentence to the days-left rule. This file's Deploys bullet and directory map still describe `binDaysLeft` as rounded up and never negative, which stays true; they were not edited, per the kickoff's scope. Not pushed (the CK-45 precedent). Check `(hi)` is Steven's: a fresh throwaway photograph sent to the bin in the deployed app reads *“…put it back for 30 more days.”*, is put back, and no count moves. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-64.1-report.md`.

else not ready yet), `already_removed`, `not_removed`, and **`not_pending` — the lost race on ALL THREE acts** (the api-reference's `_CHANGED_WHILE_DECIDING`, not a removal-specific code) → *changed while you were looking at it — refresh the list and try again*; none claims anything was deleted or put back. **Send to bin is one click, no confirmation** — it can be undone. **Put back lets the SERVER decide where the photograph goes** (the gate at restore time — never a parameter, never "back to what it was"); where the gathering requires approval (`requiresApproval`, the effective value already on the prop) a hint beside it says so **from the reader's seat** — *The host will look at it again before anyone else sees it.* for the uploader, and for the host reading their own binned row *It'll wait for you to publish or decline it before anyone else sees it.* (the CK-43.1 seat rule: never a person waiting on "the host", which would be themselves — the host-seat line is this phase's addition to the kickoff's one string, recorded); no hint in an open gathering. **Delete permanently always takes a second, deliberate step, inline** (the decline's shape; never `window.confirm`): the bin record §7.1's sentence **character for character** — *Are you sure? This will permanently remove the photo from CoveyKeep and cannot be recovered.* — with **Delete permanently** and **Keep it** beside it, the name flowing from `brand.ts` (`DELETE_PERMANENTLY_CONFIRMATION`) and the rendered sentence pinned as a literal; "from CoveyKeep" because a printed book is out of reach; the step stays open while its request runs ("Deleting…") and closes with the outcome; **the editor and the step never share a row** (opening one closes the other). **Optimistic-free, one act at a time on the existing `actingId` guard** — plus `actingAct`, so the review's Publish label reads "Publishing…" only during a publish (until now it read so during ANY act on the row — unreachable before a bin act could share the row; a label fix, no review copy or behaviour changed); a success re-reads and the server's body renders; **a destroyed row leaves the list** (a row in destruction is visible to nobody) and a polite `role="status"` line, *Photo deleted.*, says why, cleared by the next act or the next re-read the person asks for (a poll tick is neither); a refusal stays on the row with **Refresh the list**. **THE BIN'S LINE** (`mediaStateMessage`'s `removed` branch): *In your bin. Only you can see it, and you can put it back for N more days.* — N from `binDaysLeft(removed_at, now)`, `REMOVED_BIN_DAYS` against an injectable `now`, **rounded UP to whole days**, *for 1 more day*, *for less than a day* in the last day, never negative, and no count at all for a row with no readable stamp; **it never says the photograph will be deleted, emptied or cleared after the window** — the automatic clear ships switched off and nothing clears a bin until it is on; what is enforced is that the person stops seeing it and can no longer put it back, and that is all the copy claims (the kickoff's ban list — *deleted after / emptied / cleared / forever / safe / backed up* — pinned on the rendered bin row and both confirmations). **migration 0026, head 0026** — `media.removed_by_person_id`, nullable, FK → people with no delete rule, no default, NOTHING BACKFILLED; the verifier **191 → 197**, 6 new, each negative-tested on the dev DB and as a subprocess in the suite; **120 frontend tests**, one string pin changed; build + lint clean; two commits, one per root). **THE FACT**: two bins, and a photograph is in exactly one — the bin of WHOEVER REMOVED IT. `remove` writes the caller's id and `decline` the host's, in the SAME guarded UPDATE as `removed_at` (pinned at the cursor: one UPDATE on media per act names the remover, and it names the stamp); `destroy` and the sweep leave it alone — a destroyed row keeps its remover as provenance. **THE NARROWING**: `_visible_media`'s `removed` branch admits the uploader only when the remover is them — a photograph the host removed or declined is visible to NOBODY (the gathering's bin exists as a state with no reader; the host's view, host restore and co-hosts are later phases); a row with a NULL remover (every removal before 0026) is visible to nobody, deliberately — a self-removal and a decline cannot be told apart after the fact, and showing a declined photograph to its uploader is the harm the record exists to prevent; a never-published row stamped `now()` with a NULL publisher); **no count moves** (`photo_count`, `total_bytes` and the keeper's usage identical across a remove-then-restore, pinned); ONE guarded UPDATE keyed on `ready` + `removed` + removed-by-this-caller, a rowcount of 0 the lost race, refused; - **1.56.0** (2026-09-25): Phase CK-62 — **the worker says it is backing off; make that true** (check (gx)'s loose end; **code root only — `app/worker.py` and `tests/test_worker.py`; NO migration, head stays 0025**; the verifier **191 → 191, unchanged and re-run**; **466 backend tests**, 4 new, **ONE existing test edited — the idle pin gains its negative half**; frontend untouched; one commit). **THE DEFECT**: `Poll.BACKOFF` was returned on `ingest.Outcome.MISCONFIGURED` at CK-35 and referenced nowhere else in the file — `run` had no branch for it, so it fell through to `await sleep(_idle_delay())`, 5 s ± 1 s, **identical to an empty queue** — while the enum comment said *sleep, retry, shout*, the log line said *the worker is backing off*, and the module docstring said the backoff was *so a mistyped dashboard value does not hammer the store*. The docstring was right about the mechanism and wrong about the consequence: backing off like an empty queue IS hammering the store when only a human editing a dashboard can clear the condition — the two halves in tension, each fine alone, which is how it survived CK-35 → CK-61. **(gx) measured it on 2026-09-25**: a rejected credential retried every ≈5 s for 24 minutes, the release line on every pass, ≈288 doomed store calls; **no data at risk** — `attempts` 0 and `available_at` fixed throughout, exactly as CK-61's release promises. The third surface in a week describing behaviour the code did not have (CK-55's label, CK-57's `last_error`). **THE FIX — one constant, one branch**: `MISCONFIGURED_BACKOFF = 60.0` beside `IDLE_POLL`, jittered the same way — `_jittered(interval)`, ± `IDLE_JITTER` as an absolute offset, because desynchronising workers that woke together is the same job at any interval; `_idle_delay` keeps its formula byte-for-byte and `_backoff_delay` sits beside it — and `run`'s loop tail sleeps `_backoff_delay()` on `BACKOFF` and `_idle_delay()` on everything else. **60 s is a choice, and the constant's comment says why**: the condition is human-clearable only; editing `R2_WORKER_*` on Render triggers a redeploy, so the interval is NEVER on the recovery path and costs no responsiveness; a minute cuts store traffic and log volume roughly twelvefold while still proving liveness to anyone watching. **`RETRY_BACKOFF` deliberately not reused** — per-row retry timing, not loop pacing — and its first rung being a minute too is recorded as coincidence, not coupling, with a source-level pin that `worker.py` never names it. **`NOT_READY` KEEPS THE IDLE INTERVAL, deliberately, and says so in four places** (the enum comment, rule 2 of the module docstring, the loop-tail comment, and a test): the worker is designed to start before the web service has finished migrating and to pick up as soon as the schema lands, and slowing it would make a documented behaviour worse. **The three surfaces agree**: the docstring paragraph is AMENDED IN PLACE (the CK-57 idiom — the CK-35 sentence stands verbatim, a paragraph beneath records what was true, what was not, (gx)'s measurement and what makes it true now); the enum's two comments say the interval each member sleeps; the ERROR line says *the worker polls again in about 60 s (MISCONFIGURED_BACKOFF, not the idle interval): only a dashboard edit clears this, and that edit redeploys the worker*. **The log's frequency is unchanged, on purpose**: one line per pass is right for an actively broken condition; the interval already made it a line a minute; rate-limiting the line is a separate question, recorded in the comment above it. **Tests** (`test_worker.py`, the injected-`sleep` pattern): the constants — 60.0, the idle pair untouched, the two bands disjoint with room, `worker.py`'s source never naming `RETRY_BACKOFF`; **the (gx) shape through `run`** — a bodiless 401 on every HEAD, four passes, every sleep inside the backoff band and outside the idle band, the row `uploaded` / attempts 0 / `claimed_at` None / `available_at` unchanged / the reason written, and four ERROR lines — one per pass — each naming the interval and carrying no key id; The set stays named-only (its comment now says so and why); the numeric knowledge lives in the helper alone, whose docstring carries (gv)'s measurement and the reason a third classifier must call it rather than copy a mixed set. **`'400'` STAYS OUT, and the docstring says why**: a key id of the wrong LENGTH draws `InvalidArgument` / 400 — R2 length-validates before it authenticates ((gv)'s first run) — a malformed request and not a credential fact; admitting 400 would release every genuinely bad request forever. **`_ABSENT_CODES` annotated, not churned** — membership and behaviour byte-identical; the comment names it the superseded mixed form, right and left alone (storage.py tests its own copy on the HEAD, ingest.py's on the GET after it — both forms in both places because the shape is the call's), and says a third classifier reads the status the helper's way. **The module docstring's THIRD CLASS paragraph is AMENDED IN PLACE** (the CK-57 idiom): the original promise stands verbatim, and a paragraph beneath it says what was true, what was not, the measurement, and what makes it true now. **THE OTHER HALF — the three access key ids are shape-checked at boot**: `config.py` gains `_r2_access_key_id_shape` (exactly 32 hexadecimal characters, either case, no stripping — a stray space IS a mistyped value) behind a `field_validator` on `r2_upload_access_key_id` and `r2_serve_access_key_id` (web `Settings`) and on `r2_worker_access_key_id` (`WorkerSettings`) — the `database_url` pattern already in both classes; the refusal names the field, the shape and the LENGTH and never the value (the CK-36 rider's rule, kept by the message itself as well as by `hide_input_in_errors`). The module's own rule applied: fail at boot in front of whoever deployed rather than at the first claim; the upload and serve ids too, because a malformed one there fails a person's upload rather than the queue. **The secret keys are deliberately NOT shape-checked and a test pins that** — nothing has measured their format. **A real boot proves it**: `python -m app.worker --once` with a 33-character id exits 1 with *r2_worker_access_key_id … exactly 32 hexadecimal characters; this value has 33*, the value absent from the output. **THE STOP CONDITION, reported not silenced**: (gv)'s fingerprint measured the worker's id at length 32; the upload and serve ids are UNMEASURED from this machine (dashboard values), so the deploy itself is their measurement — a web-service boot failure naming either field is that finding, and the constraint is not to be loosened for it. **Tests** — `test_worker.py`: `_botocore_error(status, *, body, operation)`, a `ClientError` built by running **botocore's own REST-XML parser** over a raw response (never by hand — a bodiless response → the bare status, an XML body → the named code), beside the hand-built `_client_error` whose comment now says it is why 451 green tests never caught this (a stub that encodes the assumption of the code under test proves nothing); THE COUPLING IT CORRECTS: `api/media.py` had never imported `ingest` until CK-58, and importing it pulls `services/processing.py` — Pillow and pillow-heif — into the web process, a codec stack the API never executes. Measured on the API's `import app.main`: **21 PIL modules plus pillow-heif at HEAD, zero after the move; `app.services.processing` and `app.services.ingest` no longer in the API's import graph**; resident memory on this Windows machine 97.7 MB → 95.0 MB working set after the import (four runs each; the Linux figure will differ, and the import graph is the fact). **The boundary follows the CALLER SET**: `destruction.py` holds what the API and the worker both call — the mark, and the sweep — needs the models and sqlalchemy alone, and **must never import `ingest`, `processing`, `storage` or botocore** (a Gotcha below); `handle_destroying` stays in `ingest.py`, worker-only. **`ingest.py` re-exports the name** so the ladder reads whole from the module that runs it and CK-58's three pins resolve `ingest.mark_for_destruction` unedited — the kickoff's proof: **437 passed with `git diff --stat backend/tests/` empty**, then committed alone as `6eb2f3a`. **THE SWEEP — `services/destruction.py::sweep_expired_bin(db, now, *, limit) -> int`**: SELECT at most `limit` rows that are `ready` AND `removed` AND `removed_at IS NOT NULL` AND `removed_at <= now − REMOVED_BIN`, oldest `removed_at` first, then **`mark_for_destruction` on each row individually, committing per row, counting the Trues** — **ROW BY ROW, NEVER A BULK UPDATE**: the statement's own `status = 'ready'` guard is what makes a second marker's rowcount 0 (two worker instances are one `numInstances` line away), and it only works per row; the SELECT takes no lock, because a lock would be a second safety mechanism doing the guard's job; the rows are expunged before the loop so the per-row commits never expire them (an expired attribute on an async session is a lazy load that cannot run). **It marks; CK-54's worker step destroys** — no `delete_published_object` call, no second routine, no second decrement. **The two clocks meet at one instant**: `_visible_media` shows a removed row while `removed_at > now − REMOVED_BIN`, the sweep takes it once `removed_at <= now − REMOVED_BIN` — one number (`retention.REMOVED_BIN`), two readers, no gap and no overlap, pinned. **No memorial branch, no type branch, no account branch, no fixture exemption** (§7.4, §8 — each decided, each pinned). **THE SEVENTH WORKER VARIABLE, and the amendment is deliberate**: `WorkerSettings.sweep_enabled: bool = False` — `WorkerSettings`' docstring, `config.py`'s module docstring, `render.yaml`'s worker comment and the field-list test all said *six values, and exactly six*, and all are amended in the same commit with the reason: **the six-value rule exists so the worker can never name the upload or serve credential, and a boolean grants no powers** — the credential sentence is untouched, the pin still asserts no field names a credential or the session secret, and the six-variable subprocess boot test is unchanged because **a worker booted with exactly the six IS the dark sweep**. `render.yaml` declares `SWEEP_ENABLED` with `value: "false"` — **a quoted string, one divergence from the kickoff's `value: false`**: blueprint values are strings and a YAML boolean is a type surprise on sync; pydantic reads `"false"` and `"true"` as the boolean either way, pinned. **THE WIRING (`worker.py::run`)**: `sweep_enabled: bool = False` and `clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic` join `sleep` as injectable arguments; a pass runs only when `poll_once` returned IDLE — **never between drains** — and `SWEEP_INTERVAL` has elapsed on `clock` since the last pass (the first pass at the first idle poll after boot, so an operator who turns the switch on sees the line at once); a pass that marked anything is followed by another poll, not a sleep, so its marks are drained before the loop sleeps — which makes **`--once` with the switch on the operator's sweep from a shell**; a pass that fails (schema not there, or a bug) is logged and retried at the next interval, never a hot loop, never the loop's death; the startup line says `sweep=on|off`. **`SWEEP_INTERVAL = 15 minutes` and `SWEEP_BATCH = 20`, and why**: the clock being swept is thirty days, so any interval in minutes is invisible to a person, and fifteen makes a backlog visibly move within the hour; the batch bounds what a pass puts AHEAD of a fresh upload — `claim_next` orders by `created_at` across both ladders, so a marked row, older than any new upload, is claimed first — at ≈0.55 s per destruction (CK-56's measured window) twenty is ≈11 s, inside record §6.3's 60 s p95 with margin; 1,920 a day exceeds any plausible bin exit rate by orders of magnitude, and a ten-thousand backlog (binned at once, then a month) drains in about five days, Phase C's empty-the-bin being the path for that case. **ONE INFO line per pass that marked anything** — `bin sweep: N photograph(s) marked for destruction; their retrieval window has closed` — the count and nothing else; a pass that marked nothing logs nothing. **Tests** — `tests/test_destruction.py` +6: the boundary by an injected `now` (one second inside left, the window's own instant and one second past swept; publication state and removal stamp untouched, job columns reset, the three layers left for the worker, both invariants re-run); **what the sweep takes is exactly what the uploader has stopped seeing**; the limit taking the oldest first; a `removed` row on EVERY non-ready rung never selected and nothing written; a memorial's bin sweeping exactly as a potluck's; **two sweeps over one expired row marking it once through the real guard** (the other sweep run to completion between the outer's SELECT and its mark, from a second session; one decrement; the endpoint afterwards drawing the 404). `tests/test_worker.py` +5: the numbers, and the switch reading a dashboard string both ways; **THE FLAG-OFF PROOF** — the loop driven past four whole intervals against an expired removed row with the switch at its default, the function stubbed to fail if reached and **no statement at the cursor carrying the sweep's predicate** (the flag-on test asserts the predicate IS emitted, which is what makes the negative mean something), row and counters untouched; flag on — the first idle poll sweeps, the mark is claimed and destroyed (objects gone, derivative rows gone, counters 0) before the first sleep, fourteen idle cycles pass with no pass, the fifteenth minute passes once more marking nothing and logging nothing, and the one INFO line is the exact text with no id in it; **the queue drains before the sweep** — two uploads ahead of an expired row under `--once`: PROCESSED, PROCESSED, IDLE, sweep, PROCESSED, IDLE, exit 0, no sleep; a failing pass logged twice (a `ProgrammingError`, then a bug) with the worker still polling. **DIVERGED FROM THE KICKOFF, recorded**: (1) **the prerequisite was NOT met** — the Hub's `WORKING-ON-NOW.md` was dirty at 1.127.0 (the planning session's own entry) when the phase began; the CK-60 precedent applied: proceeded with the file untouched, the docs commit staging its files by path, and the breach heads the report; (2) **STEP 1 could not be performed from this machine** — the deployed database's connection string is a live credential this session does not hold; the report carries what is reachable (the gathering id, the filenames, the archived check outcomes, the exact query) and the psql capture is Steven's, BEFORE the switch is turned on — shipping off is what keeps that ordering honest; **and the date: `removed_at` 2026-09-14 + 30 days = 2026-10-14, so a switch turned on before then marks nothing, and the two rows go on the first pass at or after 2026-10-14**; (3) the test plan's `grep -rn 'processing' backend/app/api/` returns four hits, all comments cross-referencing `processing.DECODABLE_FORMATS` / `MAX_PIXELS` (the API's allowlist must agree with the decoder, and those comments say so) — the proof is the import graph, not the grep, and it is clean; (4) `value: "false"` above; (5) `reference/operations/deploy.md` was not on the kickoff's docs list and states *six environment variables, and exactly six* — the operator's doc for the very dashboard step 6 uses, amended at 1.9.0; (6) the first heredoc'd edit script failed to parse in this Bash environment and nothing ran — every edit was re-applied from a `.py` file run by path, and every replacement asserted an exact count. - **1.50.0** (2026-09-23): Phase CK-56 — **the claim clock admits the second ladder** (a verifier fix and one Gotcha; **no migration — head stays 0025**; the verifier at **190, unchanged** — one assertion narrowed, none added, removed or renumbered; **434 backend tests**, unchanged and re-run; frontend untouched; nothing under `app/` changed — the destroy path, the worker, `_claim` and `_settle` have no diff). **THE DEFECT, CONFIRMED ON THE DEPLOY AND NOT INFERRED**: `claim_mismatch` asserted `(status = 'processing') <> (claimed_at IS NOT NULL)` — an iff written when `processing` was the only claimed rung — and CK-54 added a second ladder whose rows `_claim` stamps through the same unconditional line, because a destroy job is claimed IN PLACE (`destroying` is both the queue and the claim). So **any `destroying` row fails it while a worker holds it**. Check `(gj)` planted that state on the deploy on 2026-09-23 and read **189 passed, 1 failed** on exactly that assertion, exit 1; restoring the row returned 190/0. **The window is ~543 ms per row, which is why CK-54's and CK-55's runs all missed it, and it does not stay small**: a stalled claim holds the stamp until `RECLAIM_AFTER` and needs a live worker to clear it, so a worker dying mid-destruction fails the verifier indefinitely, and Phase B's bulk sweep turns a negligible window into a routine one — the same cost CK-55 was written to remove, so it lands before Phase B rather than after. **THE NARROWING KEEPS TWO IMPLICATIONS RATHER THAN WIDENING THE EQUALITY, because it is NOT an iff on the second rung**: `processing` still implies a stamp, but a `destroying` row legitimately has none when freshly marked (the API marks straight to the rung with the job columns reset) and when it has dead-lettered (the ladder is spent and the stamp is cleared) — criterion `(status = 'processing' AND claimed_at IS NULL) OR (claimed_at IS NOT NULL AND status NOT IN ('processing', 'destroying'))`, label *claimed_at is always set on a processing row, and set only on a processing or destroying one*, the detail string and the variable name kept. Everything the old line caught is still caught: a stamp stranded on an `uploaded`, `ready`, `failed` or `destroyed` row, and a `processing` row without one. **THE SECOND ASSERTION THIS PHASE WAS COMMISSIONED TO ADD WAS NOT ADDED, and the omission is recorded in the comment block as chosen** — the count is 190, not 191. The gap is real: a spent destruction stays at `destroying` by design (CK-54 refused `failed`, which would claim the wrong job failed and would make the row visible again), so the existing `failed`/`last_error` line never sees it, and `(gj)` confirmed a `destroying` row at `attempts = 3` with `last_error` NULL fires nothing. **Both candidate criteria were rejected, the first on evidence gathered here**: the constant-free form (`attempts > 0 AND claimed_at IS NULL`) reads as strictly stronger and is not — `_release_misconfigured` settles only `status` and `claimed_at`, so a row abandoned once (the attempt counted, and BELOW the bound no `last_error` written — the claim merely re-stamped) and then released on a rejected credential sits at `destroying`, attempts 1, no stamp, no reason, **three layers intact and claimable**, which is healthy and matches; run through the real code paths and fixtures that state made the constant-free criterion count 1 and the mirrored one 0. The kickoff's reasoning was per-call and the violation is per-reachable-state, two calls apart. Mirroring `ingest.MAX_ATTEMPTS` was rejected too, by Steven: the line has **no reachable violation today** (every path to the bound writes a reason), so it is a regression guard rather than a detector and nothing loses coverage by waiting, and a mirrored bound fails SILENTLY — lowered it goes vacuous while reading as coverage, raised it fires on that released row — where `EXPECTED_REVISION` is mirrored but asserted against the database, so its drift is loud. **The real question is a code one — whether `_release_misconfigured` should write a neutral reason, or clear a stale one, so a `destroying` row's `last_error` means one thing — and once it is settled the assertion writes itself without a bound. That is CK-57, and it is a code phase.** **Negative-tested three ways on the local dev DB at 0025** (the CK-54/CK-55 practice; the empty DB needs the full accounts → people → gatherings → media chain with `uploaded_at` set, or the CK-34 assertion fails instead and the plant proves nothing): `destroying` + a claim stamp — the `(gj)` state — → **190/0**, with the OLD criterion run by hand against that same plant returning **1** and the new one **0**, so the narrowing is what changed the answer; `destroyed` + a claim stamp → **189/1** on the narrowed line, exit 1, so it did not blanket-exempt a stamp; `processing` + no stamp → **189/1**, so the surviving implication survives; every plant removed, every table back to zero, 190/0, exit 0. STEP 0: both roots clean, head 0025, 434 tests, the Hub's 1.79.0 / 1.118.0 committed by Steven as `d04db38` before the phase began. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent.
