- **1.49.0** (2026-09-23): Phase CK-55 — **the CK-41 assertion, narrowed for the destruction ladder** (`decisions/2026-09-20-the-bin-counts-and-empties.md` §7.2; a verifier fix and one Gotcha; **no migration — head stays 0025**; the verifier at **190, unchanged** — no assertion added, removed or renumbered; **434 backend tests**, unchanged and re-run; frontend untouched; nothing under `app/` changed). **THE FINDING IT CLOSES**: CK-54's dev-deploy verification passed six of seven checks and failed (gf) for real — the deployed verifier read **189/1**, the one failure CK-41's *every live media row is ready* on the three photographs CK-54's verification destroyed, each `status = destroyed` with `publication_state = live`. **That is exactly what destruction is designed to leave**: `destroy` never touches `publication_state`, because bin record §7.2 keeps the emptied row and its words for the audit trail and `remove` is the act that owns that column; the row is made unreachable through `status` instead — `_visible_media`'s one `status` term. So the assertion's rationale (*a `live` row in any other rung means something wrote around the worker — a published photograph nothing can serve*) was retired by design at CK-54, which narrowed CK-32's derivative assertion for exactly this reason and left this one standing; **both count invariants pass**, so the shape CK-54 was chosen for is intact, and what was too wide was the claim that nothing needed rewriting. **The criterion becomes `status NOT IN ('ready', 'destroying', 'destroyed')` — BOTH rungs**, because a `live` + `destroying` row is legitimate for at least a worker cycle and indefinitely if the destruction dead-letters, and a verifier that failed inside that window would be a false alarm by construction. **The label is rewritten in the same edit**: its parenthetical had become precisely false of a `live` + `destroyed` row (that row *is* in the `live` state and has no layers at all), and an assertion that passes while printing a false sentence is worse than one that fails, because a failure is loud — *every live media row is ready or in the destruction ladder (nothing is published without its layers unless it is being destroyed)*, detail *N live media row(s) in neither the ready rung nor the destruction ladder*, `live_unready` kept. **No new assertion, and why** (the comment block says it in the CK-32/CK-54 house form): the excluded set already carries a STRICTER guarantee than this line ever gave it — *no destroyed photograph still has derivative rows*, CK-54's terminal half — which is the difference from CK-54's narrowing of CK-32, where the terminal half had to be created; the `DELIBERATELY NOT ASSERTED` paragraph above the line is preserved verbatim (a different question, still true). **The destroy path is untouched**: moving `publication_state` on destruction would collide with §7.2 and with what `remove` means; `api/media.py`, `ingest.py`, `storage.py` and `worker.py` have no diff. **Negative-tested both directions on the local dev DB at 0025** (the CK-54 practice — one plant passes and the other fails): a planted `live` + `destroyed` row with no derivative rows → **190/0**, and the OLD criterion run by hand with that plant in place returns **1** (the new one 0), so the narrowing is what changed the answer; a planted `live` + `uploaded` row → **189/1** on this line with the new detail, exit 1; both plants removed, every table back to zero, 190/0, exit 0. **The Gotcha, and this file's first Gotchas section**: `PUBLISHED_PREFIX = "media/"` — the constant's name says published and its value does not; `published` is the bucket, `media/` the key prefix, a published object's key `media/<media_id>/<layer>` — a check in CK-54's verification was written against the wrong path. **Why this lands before Phase B**: until it does, every deployed verifier run reports 189/1 forever, which costs an operator the one thing the verifier is for — telling a real breakage from a known one. STEP 0: both roots clean, head 0025, 434 tests collected, the Hub's 1.76.0 / 1.115.0 already committed by Steven. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent; (gg) and (gh) ran here, and the push with the deployed re-run on the three real rows is check (gi). Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-55-report.md`.
- **1.48.0** (2026-09-22): Phase CK-54 — **the bin arc, Phase A: a photograph's bytes can finally be destroyed** (`decisions/2026-09-20-the-bin-counts-and-empties.md` §6/§7.1/§7.2, all three BUILT here; **backend only; migration 0025, head 0025**, `alembic check` clean; the verifier at **190** (187 + 3, each negative-tested); **434 backend tests**, 25 new; frontend untouched — no route calls either endpoint). **THE FACT THIS PHASE RETIRES: nothing in CoveyKeep had ever deleted a published photograph's bytes.** `storage.py` carried one delete function and it was for quarantine originals; "remove" set `publication_state = 'removed'`, stamped `removed_at`, left `status = 'ready'`, and the three layers stayed in R2 forever — which is why neither `total_bytes` nor `photo_count` had ever been decremented, and why an allowance was a limit on what an account had *ever* kept. **Phase A of three: A (this) → B (the 30-day sweep) → C (the bin surface, which any user-facing quota surface is still gated on).** **Migration 0025, additive**: `media_status` grows to seven — **`destroying`** (claimable, retryable; `processing`'s mirror, except that `claimed_at` NULL means "marked, never claimed" because this rung is both the queue and the claim) and **`destroyed`** (terminal; `ready`'s mirror) — appended after `failed` with `ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS` and **used by nothing in the same transaction** (the CK-32 trap, which aborts a deploy and passes in the suite, because a fresh test DB creates the type in the same transaction); the downgrade drops neither label and says why (there is no honest way to reverse a destruction). **`storage.py::delete_published_object`** — the first function in this product that destroys a photograph's stored bytes: `put_published_object`'s guard (**the `WorkerClient` alone**, the one credential scoped to write that bucket — the UploadClient cannot reach it and the ServeClient is read-only, which CK-33's verifier proves against live R2) and `delete_quarantine_object`'s semantics (**an absent object is a SUCCESS**, which here is not a nicety but what makes the retry idempotent). **THE API MARKS AND THE WORKER DESTROYS, and the decrement rides the marking statement** — `POST /media/{id}/destroy` moves a `ready` row to `destroying` and drops `photo_count` and `total_bytes` in **ONE statement** (the mirror of CK-51a's increment; the byte figure summed from the row's own derivative rows inside it; **not clamped at zero** — the invariant is exact, so a negative is a finding and a floor would hide only the case where it came out right), and the job columns are reset so a photograph that took two ingest attempts still gets three destruction attempts. **NO VERIFIER INVARIANT CHANGED, and that is the argument the shape was chosen for**: the row leaves `ready` in the transaction that decremented, so `photo_count` = the count of `ready` rows and `total_bytes` = the sum over their layers both stay true — a destruction that needed a new invariant would be a destruction with a new way to be wrong. **THE ORDER INVERTS CK-36's, DELIBERATELY** (`ingest.py::handle_destroying`, stated in the code with its reason because the next reader will otherwise "fix" it): publish commits the DB *before* deleting the quarantine original, because the DB is the record of what exists and a lingering original is cheaper than a lost photograph; **destruction deletes the three published objects FIRST and commits `destroyed` after**, because commit-first plus a failed delete leaves a row claiming destruction while its bytes sit in the bucket — *after the product told someone it was gone*. Delete-first fails safe: a crash leaves `destroying`, the retry finds the objects absent, and absent-is-success makes it idempotent for free (pinned from a second session at the instant of each delete — the mirror of CK-36's ordering pin). **The derivative rows are read here and deleted in the terminal transaction, never at the mark**: their `storage_key` is what was actually written, and keeping them through `destroying` is what makes a stuck row legible to an operator. **TWO ENDPOINTS, NOT ONE FLAG** (confirmed by Steven 2026-09-22): `POST /media/{id}/remove` (to bin — **destroys nothing and frees nothing**: the layers stay, the row stays `ready`, the photograph goes on counting, bin record §3) and `POST /media/{id}/destroy` (immediate, irreversible, and it reaches a `removed` row too — the per-photograph *empty the bin*). A `permanent: true` flag means one client bug permanently destroys what should have gone to the bin, and there is no restore. **Both act on a `live` row, so this IS the takedown of a published photograph** — `media-pipeline.md` §11's item, **BUILT** — arriving here rather than in a phase of its own; **`decline` is unchanged** (pending-only, a `live` row still 409 `not_pending`): decline is the REVIEW's bottom, and two acts reaching `removed` from different places is the honest shape. **Authorization: the gathering's HOST, and the UPLOADER for their own — NOT THE KEEPER** (bin record §7.1; the removal rule — a keeper holds a reference, never a right to publish or destroy); a keeper, an accepted invitee and a stranger each draw the media 404 byte-identical to a missing id, **pinned with the keeper first shown to be inside the read audience** so the 404 is the rule and not a broken fixture. **`_visible_media` gains its one and only `status` term**: **a row in destruction is visible to NOBODY.** Bin record §7.2 keeps a destroyed row's filename, caption and tags for the audit trail (*a photograph captioned "Jenny at bat" was deleted* records what was lost where *something was deleted* does not) on the stated ground that the words are unreachable, and says that "follows from the row leaving `ready`" — **it does not follow on its own**, because every other branch of that criterion turns on `publication_state`, so a destroyed `live` row would have gone on being listed, searched and patched; writing it into the one criterion every read, the words patch and the review acts apply is what makes the requirement structural, and it also makes a destroy immediate from the caller's side (a second destroy draws the 404, not a refusal). **A spent destruction STAYS at `destroying`** with its `last_error` — never `failed`, which is the ingest ladder's terminal and would both claim the wrong job failed and **make the row visible again to the person who destroyed it** — kept out of the claim predicate by `attempts < MAX_ATTEMPTS`; uncounted, seen by nobody, owing a bucket operation an operator can retry. **A bug the tests caught**: `_release_misconfigured` hard-coded `uploaded`, right for an ingest claim and catastrophic for a destroy claim — it would have handed an already-uncounted photograph back to the INGEST ladder, which HEADs a quarantine original deleted at `ready`, dead-lettered it to `failed`, and made it visible again; it now releases to the rung the claim came from. **The verifier → 0025/190**: the two labels (negative-tested by `ALTER TYPE … RENAME VALUE`, the CK-36 precedent) and **"no destroyed photograph still has derivative rows"** — the destruction's whole point as a property of the data; **CK-32's "no derivative row hangs off a photograph that is not `ready`" is NARROWED to allow `destroying`**, with its reason in the source (the one rung at which a derivative row legitimately outlives `ready`, because the objects go before the commit), proven both ways — a `destroying` row with a layer passes, a `destroyed` one FAILs. **Tests**: `tests/test_destruction.py` (new, 24) — the one-statement decrement with the verifier's two invariants re-run as SQL after every mark, the keeper's refusal in both acts with nothing moved, the immediate invisibility from every seat and on every act, the surviving words with every search that cannot reach them, `remove` freeing nothing, `destroy` reaching the bin, `decline` unchanged, the delete-before-commit ordering observed from a second session, the idempotent retry over absent objects, the transient ladder ending at `destroying`, the rejected credential releasing to the right rung, the reclaim, the lost claim, and the log carrying no key and no word; one in `test_media_intents.py` (the singular bin form). **Three prior tests changed by the enum growing, each for the shape**: the ladder pin gains the two labels, the every-rung list test is narrowed to the ingest ladder (the destruction rungs are the deliberate exception to "every rung is listed"), `test_photo_count`'s head assertion is parameterised. **Riders** (bin record §6.1, plus four sites the fifth doc audit found that the record did not name): *"the 30-day bin"* named the wrong clock in **seven of the eight** places it appears and now says *the bin* (removed and still stored, whatever its age), reserving 30 days for the contributor's RETRIEVAL window — `tests/test_review.py`'s occurrence genuinely IS that window and is left; **`account_bin_count`'s query is byte-identical to HEAD** and must never grow a `removed_at` term; and the keeper's bin refusal had no singular branch, so one binned photograph read *"1 of them are in the bin"* — both forms now pinned. **Not built**: the sweep (Phase B — nothing here stamps a row into `destroying` on a clock, and it builds the CLOCK and nothing else; a second destruction would be the defect this split prevents), the bin surface and its empty-the-bin action (Phase C, account-level), restore-from-bin (§7.3), a per-account bin ceiling (§7.5), any fixture-shaped branch in the destruction routine (§8). **Recorded rather than left to be discovered**: a host who chooses *Send to bin* cannot then destroy that row, because CK-37 decided the bin is the uploader's — and that audience was NOT widened to smooth the sequence. **The clock**: CK-43's and CK-44's declines (2026-09-14, the project's only decline evidence) enter the invisible-and-still-charged state on **2026-10-14**; Phase B captures what they prove before it takes them. STEP 0: both roots clean, head 0024, 409 tests, 187 assertions — all as the kickoff said. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-54-report.md`. *(Annotated at 1.49.0: "NO VERIFIER INVARIANT CHANGED" is half true — the two COUNT invariants held on the deploy, and CK-41's live-row assertion, which is not one, failed for real on the three destroyed rows (189/1); it is narrowed at CK-55. The entry stays as written.)*
- **1.47.0** (2026-09-21): Phase CK-51b.1 — **the refusal's disclosure, split by audience: the room and the bin are the keeper's to see** (`decisions/2026-09-20-photographs-are-the-currency.md` 2.6.0 §3 and `decisions/2026-09-20-the-bin-counts-and-empties.md` 1.1.0 §5, both BUILT here; **backend only; NO migration — head stays 0024**, `alembic check` clean, no new file under `alembic/versions/`; the verifier at **187, unchanged and re-run**; **409 backend tests**, 2 new; frontend untouched; committed on top of CK-51b and NOT pushed — one push, check (fv), carries both). **THE RULE: WHO IS ASKING decides how much a refusal says, and actionability decides it** — only the keeper can free room or empty a bin. CK-51b built §3/§5's copy as its kickoff specified and reported that it retired CK-50's pin against naming the headroom ("usage in disguise"); it did, and CK-50 was right: to any caller but the keeper the room left and the bin count are unusable, *"empty the bin to make room"* offered to them is copy naming an affordance they cannot use (the defect CK-50 refused when it declined keeper v2 §6's claim-flow sentence), and the bin count is a fact about another person's deletions. `api/media.py::_enforce_limits` gains **`caller_account_id: UUID`** (the id, not the `AuthContext` — the function stays DB-shaped; `create_intents` passes `ctx.person.account_id`) and, after the quota refuses, compares it against `resolution.keeper_account_id` — the comparison `_gathering_for_read` already makes, not a second one: **the keeper** reads CK-51b's two forms unchanged (the bin clause where `account_bin_count > 0`, else the room-left / no-room-left form); **everyone else** reads ONE form — *this space is full — it holds 10,000 photos, and you're adding 3* — no room figure, no bin count, no direction naming whose space it is, **and `account_bin_count` is not called on that path at all** (a query whose answer may not be shown is not run). Untouched: the memorial branch (its subject is the gathering's own count, never an account's), the keeperless 422 verbatim, the lock, the 422 field paths, every count. **Tests** (`tests/test_media_intents.py`, 34 → 36): **CK-50's pin restored** for the non-keeper — `test_a_quota_refusal_never_discloses_usage_or_whose_storage_it_is` asserts the exact withheld form and that the room figure, the usage figure, `bin`, `host`, `keeper` and every byte unit are absent, with a comment saying the pin is CK-50's, restored here, and why it had moved; the keeper's two assertions kept, each with a comment saying the disclosure is permitted because it is the caller's own space and only they can act on it, and each now proving the caller keeps the gathering; **the four-case matrix** (keeper / non-keeper × bin empty / bin not empty, one gathering, an accepted invitee as the non-keeper) by exact copy, the withheld form identical in both bin states; **a host who is not the keeper** (the sponsorship shape, the column moved) reading the withheld form in both bin states while the keeper reads their own room and bin on the same gathering; `account_bin_count` stubbed to raise and never reached on the non-keeper refusal, the accepted path, the memorial refusal or the keeperless refusal. **The copy matrix, verbatim** — keeper, bin empty: *this space holds 10,000 photos and has room for 2 more, and you're adding 3* (or *…and has no room left, and you're adding 1*); keeper, bin not empty: *this space is full — 10,000 photos, and 200 of them are in the bin. Empty the bin to make room.*; not the keeper, either bin state: *this space is full — it holds 10,000 photos, and you're adding 3*. **Open, recorded in both records**: an ORGANIZATION keeper leaves every caller reading the withheld form, the org's admin included (trigger: the org member surface). STEP 0: both roots clean, head 0024. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent; the push is check (fv), and (fy) is this phase's own deploy check. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-51b.1-report.md`.

- **1.44.0** (2026-09-19): Phase CK-50 — **the gate moves to the keeper: same mechanism, new subject** (`decisions/2026-09-17-keeper-model-v2.md` 1.6.0 §6 — BUILT; **backend only; NO migration — head stays 0023**, `alembic check` clean, no new file under `alembic/versions/`; the verifier at **180, unchanged** — the schema did not change; **391 backend tests**, 4 net new; frontend untouched). **THE RULE, AND IT IS THE WHOLE PHASE — a subject swap, nothing else.** `api/media.py::_enforce_limits` (the kickoff called it `_check_quota_and_ceiling`; there is no function by that name — one gate, the swap made there) charged `gatherings.host_account_id`: right under the old model, wrong once host and keeper are two facts (v2 §7 — a person who merely runs the day must not pay for bytes they do not keep). Now it resolves the keeper through `services/keeping.py::resolved_keeper_of` — one lookup, the existing path, no second resolution — locks THAT account's row `FOR UPDATE` exactly as the host's was, and checks `account_usage` against `account_quota` for it. **Untouched**: the lock's purpose (serialise the check-and-insert so two concurrent batches cannot both pass against the same headroom), the batch-level 422 on `["body","items"]`, the memorial branch (its ceiling never consulted an account and still does not — the memorial 5 GB is compared against the memorial's own bytes), and **the reserved quantity — still the declared byte count**. **The refusal becomes keeperless, not hostless**: a gathering that resolves to no keeper (both keeper columns NULL — §5's Unkept rung) refuses on `["path","gathering_id"]` with copy that says what is true and stops — *nobody keeps this gathering, so photos can't be added to it* — no claim-flow copy, the claim surface being unbuilt; **a memorial refuses the same way** — an unkept gathering is read-only whatever its type, and the memorial branch sits after the subject check exactly as it sat after the host check (a judgment call, recorded); **a hostless gathering with a keeper is uploadable** — the CK-34 refusal closed by the gate, not the claim flow. **The quota refusal names neither host nor keeper**: *the space this gathering is kept in is limited to 10 GB, and these 3 photos (12 MB) would go past it* — the caller may be neither, and whose storage it is is not theirs to learn; **v2 §6's verbatim copy was deliberately NOT used** (its second sentence, *or you can keep it yourself and add them*, is claim-flow copy) and is the claim surface's to ship. **Tests** (`tests/test_media_intents.py`, 21 → 25): THE test that proves the phase — host and keeper two accounts, the keeper's headroom deciding in BOTH directions (keeper full + host empty → refused, no row; keeper empty + host full → 201, the reservation landing on the keeper's usage and the host's untouched — and the same host refused on the gathering they keep themselves); a keeperless potluck AND memorial (host still set) refused with the new message and no row, the message containing "keep", never "host", never a claim-flow word; a hostless gathering with a keeper accepted, the reservation on the keeper; a group gathering — own column NULL, `owning_group_id` set, constructed because Arc B has not written it — charging the GROUP's keeper (**the resolver's first rung reaching the gate for the first time**) and refused as keeperless once the group's keeper goes NULL; a memorial whose keeper (not host) is over quota still accepting, still refused above its ceiling, none of it counting against the keeper. **Prior tests changed, each with its reason**: three renamed because their names or comments said "host" where the subject is the keeper (`…the_keepers_quota`, `test_an_uploader_may_be_over_their_own_quota` — under one keeper there are no "other keepers", the uploader is an invitee — `…never_discloses_usage_or_whose_storage_it_is`, which gains "neither `host` nor `keeper` in the message"); `test_a_hostless_gathering_refuses_uploads` REPLACED by its inverse (`…with_a_keeper_is_uploadable`) and the keeperless test; every behaviour assertion beside them kept. **The wrong-subject grep**: `host_account_id` appears nowhere in `_enforce_limits`; it survives in `media.py` at `_visible_media` (the read audience's host branch — `pending` is the uploader and the host), `_is_host` and `_review_row` (the host's review, reserved to the host by design), and in `keeping.py` at `unkeep()` and `lapse_kept_statuses` (both relinquish host) — every survivor a host fact, none a quota subject. `keeping.py`'s docstring says the gate asks the resolver; `media.py`'s THE QUOTA bullet rewritten. **Not built, each with its home**: **the modeled reservation size** — a redesign, not a swap: the reservation charges the declared **original** while `total_bytes` settles at `ready` from the **derivative** rows, two different units, and CK-36 measured HEIC under-reserving by 30–54%, which makes `database-schema.md` decision 29's "over-counts in the safe direction" false — queued behind its own decision record (v2 §12 item 10; Arc B; the claim surface. - **1.42.0** (2026-09-18): Phase CK-49b — **the cutover: the keeper moves to the column, and the relation goes quiet** (the MIGRATE half of keeper model v2's expand / migrate / contract — `decisions/2026-09-17-keeper-model-v2.md` 1.4.0 §3.1; **backend only; NO MIGRATION — head stays 0022**, `alembic check` clean, the verifier at **181, unchanged**; **388 backend tests**, 3 new; 13 prior assertion lines removed or rewritten across five files; frontend untouched). **THE TWO RULES.** (1) **`kept_gatherings` STAYS, and after this phase nothing reads or writes it**: Render runs the web service's pre-deploy migration while the OLD instance is still serving, so a migration that dropped the table would leave old code 500ing on every gathering read, media read and upload intent until the new instance took over — the drop is **CK-49c** (`render.yaml` line 15; WORKING-ON-NOW 1.87.0), after re-running 0022's backfill over the rows creation wrote between 0022 and this deploy. (2) **A representation swap, not a redesign**: every function keeps its semantics and changes only where it learns the answer. **`services/keeping.py` rewritten**: `keep()` writes `gatherings.keeper_account_id` and clears the stamp (a second keep of ANY kind refused with `ValueError` — the same account twice as before, and a second account because that is a transfer, record §4, not a second keeper; returns the `OWN` resolution); `unkeep()` NULLs it, relinquishes host if held, stamps `last_keeper_left_at` exactly as before (under one keeper "the last keeper left" and "the keeper left" are one event — the stamp's meaning did not move); `account_usage()` sums `total_bytes` plus the in-flight reservation over the gatherings that RESOLVE to the account — ONE statement fetches the candidates with their two facts, and `resolve_keeper` decides row by row (the SQL WHERE is a superset filter, never the answer); the reservation stays inside it, the one quota path, and **the upload GATE still checks the host** (v2 §6 is the next numbered phase); `lapse_kept_statuses()` NULLs `keeper_account_id` wherever the deleted account held it — on gatherings AND on `groups.keeper_account_id` (an anonymized account cannot answer for a home's bytes; **no stamp on a group** — where that lives is v2 §12 item 9's group half, Arc B's, because no gathering resolves through a group until it writes `owning_group_id`) — relinquishes host everywhere, stamps each gathering that lost its keeper; the call site in `profile.py` untouched. **Two wiring helpers beside the resolver**: `resolved_keeper_of(db, gathering)` — a loaded row's two facts to `resolve_keeper` (`_gathering_for_read`) — and **`keeps_gathering(account_id, gathering_id)` — the ladder as ONE SQL criterion, `coalesce(the group's keeper, the gathering's own) = account`, for the WHERE clauses that decide which rows are fetched at all** (the list's one statement — the CK-20 statement-count pin holds — and `_visible_media`), safe only because the CHECK makes a both-set row impossible, pinned to agree with the Python resolver on every legal shape, **aliased so an outer `Gathering` never auto-correlates** (the bug it would otherwise be). **`grace_state` and `ARCHIVE_AFTER` / `DELETE_AFTER` UNTOUCHED** — annotated: inert (nothing but its own tests calls it), and v2 §5's five-rung ladder is behaviour, not representation, arriving with its surfaces (the in-app alert, the claim button, the storage screen; nothing past Frozen before export). `resolve_keeper` untouched in body and signature; its "not called by any consumer yet" paragraph corrected. **The three query sites** — `_gathering_for_read`'s kept check, the list's `kept_by_caller`, `_visible_media`'s `keeps` — ask the resolver; **the audience rule is byte-for-byte the same**: kept-or-person-invited-or-host, the three media branches (`live` → the read audience, `pending` → uploader and host, `removed` → the uploader for 30 days), 404-not-403 with one body for missing and non-permitted. **readable and not writable** — `PATCH` keeps its three-field surface, both fields draw the unknown-field 422 (CK-42's review surface ships before CK-43's override: an override that could turn review on before anything can approve would strand a gathering's photographs). **`services/ingest.py` step 4**: the gathering's setting and the host's kind read as the transaction's first statement, the gate resolved there — **at publish time, never snapshotted at intent** — and `pending → live` written in the SAME guarded statement as `ready` where open, only over `pending` (a takedown is never undone by the worker); the host finds it; a keeper finds a `live` one); per gathering; the list still ONE statement with the tags as a correlated array subquery (pinned). - **1.33.0** (2026-09-09): Phase CK-38 — **the upload surface: a photograph you can actually add, and four states you can actually see** (seventh phase of the media arc; **frontend only** — no backend file changed, no migration, head stays 0017, the verifier at 130; **the first time the arc is usable without a REST client**). New **`components/GatheringMedia.tsx`** (the Photos section of `/gatherings/:id` — for everyone in the read audience, **collapsed by default** so the detail page stays one request; the server's audience rule decides who sees anything, `isHost` only chooses the wording) and **`lib/media.ts`** (the API types, the one state-message function, and the two calls that spend a presigned URL). **The upload control**: intent → PUT → confirm per file, the batch endpoint used as a batch. Three details earned by manual runs and not re-derived: **`file.type` is empty for HEIC in the browser** (CK-36's (da)), so `declaredContentType` falls back to the extension — the declared type is signed into the presigned PUT and a wrong one reads as an R2 permissions failure; an unnameable type goes out as the generic binary type so the **server** refuses it in its own words (the client never pre-empts the allowlist; the picker's `accept` mirrors it and is never the authority — a video chosen anyway draws the server's 422 inline against the file it names, the batch refused whole, "Nothing was uploaded"); **Content-Length is never set** (forbidden in fetch — the browser sets it from the body, which is what makes the signature match); the PUT carries no Authorization header (R2 is not our API). **The presigned URL never leaves the network call**: the intent response is a local of the upload handler, spent on the PUTs and gone — state holds media ids and file names; **a layer is fetched into a `blob:` object URL** (`fetchLayerObjectUrl`: mint through our API, one fetch, `URL.createObjectURL`, revoked on unmount) so what an `<img>` and React hold carries no credential — pinned on the PUT, the thumbnail and the web layer: the URL appears in exactly one fetch call and never in the DOM. **The four states, shown honestly** — `mediaStateMessage` is **a function of `status` AND `publication_state`, never a constant**: `pending_upload` on its way / `uploaded` waiting to be prepared / `processing` being prepared, usually within a minute, longer for a big batch (record §6.3's own words) / `ready`+`pending` **who can see it** — "Only you and the host can see this" (collapsing to "Only you" when the uploader is the host; "Only grandma and you" for the host) / **`ready`+`live` "Everyone in this gathering can see it" — written now, nothing reaches it yet**, so when the publication phase implements `decisions/2026-09-09-consent-gate-defaults.md` §1 (a private, person-owned gathering does not wait for anyone — every gathering today) these rows change message with no copy rewrite / `ready`+`removed` the bin's line / `failed` "We couldn't process this photo" with **exactly one affordance, Try again, which re-uploads from the device** (record §6.5 — never a re-queue; pinned that a ready row offers none, which the first run of the test caught as a real bug). **No copy names a reviewer, an approval, or calls a pending photograph shared** — pinned at the source (comments stripped, then every string scanned); CK-37's "waiting for the host, or nothing" resolved to *nothing*, per consent-gate-defaults §9 — for a family gathering nobody will ever review it. A PUT that fails from this device overrides the server's "on its way" with "This upload didn't finish" — the device knows what the server cannot. **Polling**: while any row is non-terminal the list re-reads every 3 s (`POLL_INTERVAL_MS`; CK-36 measured ~4.7 s claim-to-ready) and stops the moment everything is `ready` or `failed`; a page with nothing in flight makes no request at all; a failed load stops polling and offers a retry. `thumbnail` in the list, `web` when opened, **`archival` never requested** (pinned). The upload hint states the real order (record §3): uploaded, then prepared, location data removed as part of that. **One dependency verifiable only on the deploy**: the blob read needs the published bucket's CORS to allow GET from the frontend origin (the quarantine bucket's has held for PUTs since CK-34; nothing records the published bucket's) — if the URL endpoint answers 200 and the thumbnail says "Couldn't show this photo just now" with a CORS error in the console, that is bucket configuration, an out-of-band fix, not a code bug (WORKING-ON-NOW check (dj)). **86 frontend tests** (17 new: `lib/media.test.ts` 8, `routes/media.test.tsx` 9; prior tests unedited — the section is collapsed, so no existing detail test sees a media request); backend untouched, 321 green; build + lint clean. Dev-deploy: the first upload through the UI as a person would, two claim-to-ready figures from the worker's `ready` line (the first production has ever emitted), a video refused through the picker, the honesty check in two parts (WORKING-ON-NOW checks (dh)–(dm)). Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-38-report.md`.
- **1.32.0** (2026-09-09): Phase CK-37 — **reading a photograph back: who may see one that nobody has approved** (sixth phase of the media arc; **backend only**; **no migration** — head stays 0017, `alembic check` clean; `verify_schema.py` stays at 130, deliberately). **Step 0 — the audience, decided and recorded before the endpoint** (`decisions/2026-09-09-who-may-see-an-unapproved-photograph.md`, docs root): every media row is `pending`, the host's approval surface is unbuilt, and letting the gathering's read audience see everything "because the gate isn't built yet" would show unapproved photographs of children to an entire gathering. So, per `publication_state`: **`live`** → the read audience (`_gathering_for_read`: host OR keeper OR accepted invitee — built and pinned though nothing is live, so the publication phase inherits it); **`pending` → the uploader and the host, and nobody else** (a keeper is not an approver; an invitation is visibility of the gathering, never of unreviewed media); **`removed`** → the uploader alone for `REMOVED_BIN` (30 days) after `removed_at`, the host excluded — the bin's read window defined before removal exists. **NOT interim.** **404-not-403 before any other refusal**, and ONE SQL criterion — `api/media.py::_visible_media`, over `media` joined to its gathering with the audience as EXISTS subqueries — that both endpoints apply; the list requires the gathering's read audience first (a stranger draws the gathering 404) and then filters, so a keeper who uploaded nothing gets `200` and `[]`. **Two endpoints**: `GET /gatherings/{id}/media` (every rung with its `status` and `publication_state`, the uploader's display name — never an email or person id — `is_own`, the label, the bin clock; **no URL**, no `last_error`) and `GET /media/{id}/url?layer=web|thumbnail` (one presigned GET through the SERVE credential — `storage.presign_read`, a per-process `ServeClient` — for the derivative row's own `storage_key`; **422 for `archival`**, the print master on Infrequent Access, never served; **409 `not_ready` carrying the rung** for anything but `ready`; the order 404 → 422 → 409 so nothing confirms a row exists or hints at its state). **No URLs in the list, by design**: minting on demand keeps a bearer credential from existing for any object nobody is about to fetch; the cost (a grid of N thumbnails is N requests, each one indexed lookup and one local HMAC) is recorded with its trigger (CK-38 finds it hurts → a batch form on the same criterion). **§9 at volume — never logged**: the module still has no logger; pinned with the root logger at DEBUG across the success path and the 404/409/422 paths — **which found a second botocore logger: `botocore.regions` prints the bucket, object key and endpoint of every request it routes at DEBUG, presigns included**; pinned above DEBUG in `storage.py` beside `botocore.auth`. **Three worker riders** (found at CK-35/CK-36 verification): the `ready` line carries **`N ms claim-to-ready`** (a monotonic clock from the start of the claim to the ready commit — record §6.3's 60 s p95 measurable from ordinary logs for the first time); **`attempts` is persisted at `ready`** (`1` after a clean run, `2` after a retry or an abandoned claim) and the log prints the row's value — the two surfaces agree; **`available_at` is NULL on every terminal rung** — `ready` and every dead-letter path (the permanent failure, the third transient failure, the third abandonment at reclaim). No verifier assertion for the riders: one would fail on the four deployed rows written before the change without anything being wrong (the two CK-36 dead-letters keep their `available_at`; the two CK-36 `ready` rows their `attempts = 0`). **321 backend tests** (10 new: `tests/test_media_reads.py` 8 — a pending photograph visible to its uploader and the host and 404 to a keeper and to an accepted invitee who are neither, first; `test_worker.py` +2 — the `ready` line and terminal rows; four existing `attempts` pins moved from 0/1 to 1/2 by the rider); frontend untouched. Dev-deploy: the audience rule against `47474f49-…`/`bb21cbe4-…` as uploader/host and as a non-uploading member, the first repeatable claim-to-ready figure, the verifier at 0017/130; **(cs) separately, after 20:51 UTC 2026-09-09** (WORKING-ON-NOW checks (dd)–(dg)). - **1.31.0** (2026-09-09): Phase CK-36 — **processing a photograph: EXIF stripped, three layers written, and the publish that never lands half-done** (fifth phase of the media arc; **backend only**; **no migration** — head stays 0017, `alembic check` clean). **Step 0, HEIC settled**: CK-34's allowlist admits `image/heic` and its refusal copy names HEIC, and Pillow cannot open HEIC without `pillow-heif` — so the iPhone default, the launch shape's primary case, would have passed the intent and dead-lettered; **`pillow-heif==1.7.0` added beside `Pillow==12.3.0`**, both justified in `requirements.txt` in the webauthn/botocore voice (codec work is never hand-rolled; the wheels bundle libheif, Render's build unchanged), and `processing.DECODABLE_FORMATS` (`JPEG|PNG|WEBP|HEIF`) is the allowlist's real-bytes counterpart — pinned that every admitted format decodes; the allowlist and the decoder may never disagree. New **`app/services/processing.py`** (pure functions over bytes — no DB, no network, no logging): open and read the **header only** so the **~50 MP guard** runs before any pixel is decoded (`MAX_PIXELS`, mirrored into Pillow's own bomb limit; a 20 KB PNG of 50.4 MP is refused, exactly 50 MP passes); the real format from the bytes (a renamed `.mov`, GIF/BMP/TIFF, random bytes, a truncated JPEG — all `Undecodable`, all **permanent**); **`ImageOps.exif_transpose` FIRST, then strip** (strip-then-serve without transposing rotates every phone portrait sideways and looks like a UI bug); **strip everything by never carrying it** — every rendition re-encoded from pixels with no `exif=`/`icc_profile=`/`xmp=` handed to the encoder and the decoded image's `info` cleared first (an EXIF block "minus GPS" is a copied block); colour kept in **pixel space** (an embedded ICC profile converted to sRGB with littlecms and then not embedded; a garbage profile ignored); the three layers per media-layers §1/§4 — **archival** ≤4000 px JPEG q90 on **`STANDARD_IA`**, **web** ≤2560 px WebP q82 on `STANDARD`, **thumbnail** ≤400 px WebP down a quality ladder to ≤30 KB — **never upscaled**, three rows regardless, the storage class recorded on the row **verbatim as R2 spells it**. **The test that matters most has a real subject**: `tests/fixtures/media/` holds a genuine JPEG with a genuine GPS IFD (a public landmark) and orientation 6 with an asymmetric pixel layout, plus a PNG and a HEIC carrying the same IFD (`make_fixtures.py` beside them records provenance); the tests first assert the fixture HAS GPS, then process it and inspect all three layers two independent ways (Pillow's EXIF reader and a raw-bytes scan) — **nothing mocked**; orientation pinned pixel by pixel (the red quadrant lands top-right; no orientation tag survives to be applied twice). **`ingest.py` — the publish transaction** (record §8's word for the worker's `ready` step; the host's publication is untouched): (1) read the original (missing → permanent; a GET 404 after the HEAD found it → the same; store silent → transient; credential rejected → released); (2) decode/transpose/strip/render (`Undecodable` → **dead-letter at attempt 1**, `last_error` in operator terms); (3) write all three objects under **`storage.published_key(id, layer)`** — `media/<id>/<layer>`, a pure function, so the writes are **idempotent by construction** (a retry overwrites its own keys, never orphans a second set); (4) **ONE transaction**: the guarded `ready` update FIRST (a claim lost to a reclaim writes nothing at all), the media id's existing derivative rows **replaced** (delete-then-insert — the `uq_media_derivatives_media_id_layer` retry path can never surface as an IntegrityError dead-letter that reads as a decode bug), **`gatherings.total_bytes` incremented by the three layers' ACTUAL stored sum** in one UPDATE; (5) **only after that commit the original is deleted** — `worker._delete_original`, a boolean, never an error for the row; a crash between 4 and 5 costs a lingering original the 24-hour rule collects, the other order would cost the photograph — **pinned from a second session at the instant of the delete** (`ready`, three rows, bytes moved). **The reservation's baton verified, not rebuilt**: `account_usage` reads the declared size before and the derivatives' actual sum after — the two differ on purpose (over-counted in the safe direction). **Deleting an absent object is a SUCCESS** (`storage.delete_quarantine_object` swallows 404/NoSuchKey; R2 answers 204 anyway) because CK-35's (cw) **measured ~61 s of two live workers per deploy** — `SKIP LOCKED` protects the row, not a delete a slow-dying predecessor issues; a delete that fails outright leaves the row `ready` with a warning. **The dead-letter's object deletion lands too** (record §6.2's "at that moment" — after the `failed` commit, on every dead-letter path incl. the third abandonment at reclaim). `publication_state` stays `pending` on every path. **CK-35's release scaffolding deleted**: `NO_PROCESSOR_RECHECK` and `Outcome.RELEASED` gone, `Outcome.READY` in their place — pinned by absence. **Rider — `hide_input_in_errors=True`** on both `SettingsConfigDict`s (pydantic's missing-field error rendered `input_value=<the raw dict>` into deploy logs; CK-33's already printed 22 hex characters of a live credential's tail; `SecretStr` would not have helped) — pinned: a fake secret present and a field missing → the secret, the host and the literal `input_value` absent from the rendered error, the field still named; **gated on the prod cutover**. **Rider found in the suite: `alembic/env.py`'s `fileConfig` disabled every already-imported app logger** for the whole session (the migration runs from a session fixture after the app modules import), so no caplog assertion could see an app log line — `disable_existing_loggers=False` now; CK-34's never-log pins are the stronger for it. `verify_schema.py` → head **0017, 130 assertions** (3 new: every `ready` photograph has exactly its three layers; archival on `STANDARD_IA`, web/thumbnail on `STANDARD`; every gathering's `total_bytes` equals the sum over its ready photographs' layers — each negative-tested with scratch rows in the test DB, exit 1, restored). **310 backend tests** (46 new: `tests/test_processing.py` 22; `test_worker.py` +13 net — the release test replaced by the publish suite, the deletes-nothing pin inverted, the any-path pin narrowed to failure paths; `test_storage.py` +11, the `botocore.auth` pin among them); frontend untouched. **Dev-deploy — the first real photograph end to end**: intent → PUT → confirm with a GPS-bearing test image, three derivative rows, three objects in `coveykeep-dev`, nothing left in quarantine for that id, `ready`/`pending`, `total_bytes` non-zero for the first time, **the web derivative downloaded and confirmed GPS-free**; `processing` observable for the first time; the two evidence rows dead-letter or process (either is a pass); the verifier at 0017/130 from the Web Shell (WORKING-ON-NOW checks (cy)–(dc)). - **1.30.0** (2026-09-08): Phase CK-35 — **the worker exists, claims, and fails correctly** (fourth phase of the media arc; **the worker's skeleton only — no image processing**: Pillow, EXIF stripping, the derivative layers and the atomic `ready` transaction are CK-36; **backend only**; **no migration** — head stays 0017, `alembic check` clean). **`render.yaml` gains a second service, `covey-keep-worker` (`type: worker`, same repo/branch/database, starter plan, `python -m app.worker`) with NO `preDeployCommand` — only the web service runs `alembic upgrade head`** (pipeline record §8: two `alembic upgrade` processes racing one database fail on the first deploy after the worker exists; a comment sits beside the line most likely to be "helpfully" added); its env is **six variables, exactly** — `DATABASE_URL`, `R2_ENDPOINT_URL`, the two bucket names, `R2_WORKER_ACCESS_KEY_ID` / `R2_WORKER_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` — and **`R2_WORKER_*` must never be added to `covey-keep-api`** (read-write on both buckets; the verifier script cannot see it in the wrong service). **The config split first, because it blocked the phase**: every web setting is required with no default and `app.config` constructed `settings` at import, so a worker importing the module for its own class could not boot without `SESSION_SECRET` and both web credentials. New **`WorkerSettings`** in `config.py` (six fields; **no field for the upload or serve credential or the session secret**; the web `Settings` class untouched and gaining no worker field — pinned on the field lists) and **the web singleton made lazy** (a module `__getattr__`; every web consumer imports `settings` at its own import, so uvicorn and alembic still fail at boot exactly as before — pinned in a subprocess); **the worker boots in a subprocess with exactly six variables set**, the web `Settings` never constructed and `app.db` never imported (pinned). `storage.py`: **`WorkerClient`**, the third type (both buckets; `worker_client` takes `WorkerSettings` and refuses the web `Settings`; refused by both presign functions — the worker has no caller to authorise; `head`/`read`/`delete_quarantine_object` accept it), and the two web factories resolve the web settings lazily on call. New **`app/services/ingest.py`** (the claim service — testable alone): claim by `SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED LIMIT 1` over `uploaded` with `available_at` NULL or past OR `processing` stalled 15 minutes, oldest `created_at` first, stamping `claimed_at` and moving to `processing` — **`pending_upload` never claimable**; **reclaim re-claims (never delete-and-recreate) and COUNTS the abandoned attempt** — a row that keeps killing the worker must exhaust its ladder, and three abandonments dead-letter at reclaim; retry `attempts` += 1 with `available_at` pushed 1m then 5m, the third failure terminal (the 15m rung defined, unreached at three attempts); **a missing quarantine object is PERMANENT and dead-letters on the FIRST attempt** (`attempts` = 1, never 3 — the case the two deployed orphans are in); **a third class the record did not name — a rejected credential (`AccessDenied`, `InvalidAccessKeyId`, `SignatureDoesNotMatch`…) — releases the row untouched and backs the loop off** (as transient it would dead-letter every queued photograph within minutes of a dashboard typo); outcomes written with a guarded update (`WHERE status='processing' AND claimed_at=<this claim>`) so a reclaimed row's late outcome is void; `last_error` in operator terms — an exception class and an S3 code at most, never a URL, a key or a key id. **Dead-lettering releases the reservation through the one quota path** (`failed` is outside `keeping.IN_FLIGHT_STATUSES`; pinned — usage drops by the row's size; no second mechanism). **A present object is RELEASED unprocessed** — back to `uploaded`, `attempts` untouched, `claimed_at` cleared, `available_at` an hour forward (`NO_PROCESSOR_RECHECK`) so no hot loop — **scaffolding with a stated expiry: CK-36 deletes the branch**; not left `processing` (the reclaim rescues it forever), not dead-lettered (a healthy photograph destroyed). New **`app/worker.py`** (the entry point): `WorkerSettings()` → engine → `WorkerClient` → the loop — claim, settle, drain while work remains, sleep 5s jittered when idle; **never crash-loops** — a poll failing on a missing table/column or an unreachable database is logged and retried (pinned against a REAL renamed column in the test DB, then restored); SIGTERM sets the stop flag; `--once` drains and exits (0 idle / 1 not ready). **The worker deletes nothing** — `ingest.py` imports no delete or read (pinned); the dead-letter's object deletion lands with CK-36. `verify_schema.py` → head **0017, 127 assertions** (2 new: `claimed_at` set iff `processing`; every `failed` row has a `last_error` — negative-tested with scratch rows in the test DB, 2 FAILs + exit 1, restored). **264 backend tests** (23 new in `tests/test_worker.py`; prior tests unedited); frontend untouched; `.env.example` says where the worker's two credential halves go (the shell, never the file). **Dev-deploy — the deliverable is the two orphaned rows**: `926ed780-…` and `806981d9-…` dead-letter on the first attempt each, the reservation releases by 4096 bytes, `5371a749-…` is untouched, neither service fails on a migration race, the worker survives a redeploy with nothing stranded, the verifier reads 0017/127 with `media rows: 3` (WORKING-ON-NOW checks (ct)–(cx)). Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-35-report.md`.
- **1.29.0** (2026-09-08): Phase CK-34 — **the upload intent and the confirm step: deciding whether to accept a photograph, and issuing the credential if so** (third phase of the media arc; the first that writes a `media` row; **backend only** — no frontend diff, the upload UI is CK-37). New router **`app/api/media.py`**: **`POST /gatherings/{id}/media/intents`** (a batch of up to 50 declared uploads — content type, byte size, optional date label; for each accepted item a `media` row at **`pending_upload`/`pending`** and a presigned PUT from `storage.presign_upload` with the **declared size signed in as Content-Length** — R2 rejects any other body, which CK-33's (cm) observed on the real store) and **`POST /media/{id}/confirm`** (the client saying "done" is not evidence: `storage.head_quarantine_object` must find the object at exactly `upload_size_bytes`, and only then does the row move `pending_upload → uploaded`, stamping `uploaded_at` and `available_at`; a missing object, a size mismatch, or a row not in `pending_upload` is a **409 with a stable code** — `object_missing` / `size_mismatch` / `not_pending`, the CK-30 marker precedent — that changes nothing; a store that does not answer is a 503 `storage_unavailable`, never a 500; only the uploader may confirm, and anyone else draws the 404 byte-identical to a missing id). **Refusals, every one a field-level 422 landing on the offending entry and leaving NO row — the batch is refused whole**: a content type outside the image allowlist (`image/jpeg|png|webp|heic|heif` — **video refused explicitly, and the Live Photo MOV is the common case**, an iPhone share sheet hands over a paired HEIC and MOV from one gesture; the allowlist is **advisory** — a renamed `.mov` passes it, CK-35's worker verifies the bytes — and no copy may claim it screens anything), a size absent / non-positive / over 25 MB, more than 50 items, an empty batch, a date that is not this gathering's. **The ~50 MP guard is NOT this phase's**: pixel dimensions are unknowable without the bytes — a CK-35 worker check, recorded as such. **The audience is `_gathering_for_read`'s (host OR keeper OR accepted invitee) — INTERIM, and recorded as interim in the api-reference with its trigger**: uploading is an *attendee* capability (participation-terminology §5) and attendance has no surface; **not gated on keeping in either direction** (an invitee with no kept row uploads — pinned). **Quota is a RESERVATION, inside `services/keeping.py::account_usage` — the one quota path, never a second**: usage is committed `total_bytes` **plus the declared size of every in-flight upload** on an account's kept gatherings, in ONE statement via a correlated subquery; **in-flight is `pending_upload`, `uploaded`, AND `processing`** — one deliberate widening of record §6.6's "pending_upload rows", because a confirmed upload whose reservation lapsed at confirm would hold real bytes in quarantine against nobody's quota until a worker that does not yet exist published it; released at `ready` (`total_bytes` takes over — **untouched by this phase**) and at `failed`. **Gated on the gathering's HOST account only**, its row locked `FOR UPDATE` for the check-and-insert so two concurrent batches cannot both pass against the same headroom; **other keepers may be over their own quota and still upload** (pinned); a refusal names the limit and the batch, **never the host's usage** (pinned — the caller may not be the host); **a hostless gathering refuses** (`loc ["path","gathering_id"]` — no quota subject; the claim flow's question, recorded). New `account_quota` (every account is on the 10 GB free tier until the storage-tier phase reads a subscription — one function to change) and `gathering_bytes` (published plus in flight, for one gathering). **The memorial ceiling, enforced for the first time anywhere: 5 GB per memorial gathering** (keeper record §9.4) — exempt from the account quota, **not exempt from its own ceiling**, compared against the memorial's own bytes with in-flight bytes counting (pinned: an over-quota host uploads to a memorial; the second batch past 30 MB under a narrowed ceiling is refused; the potluck control is refused). **Byte units are decimal everywhere** (`1 GB = 10^9` — what the records, the consumer convention, and R2's pricing mean). **The reap — `purge_stale`'s fifth customer, and the first content table it has ever touched**: `pending_upload` rows are reaped **24 hours** after intent (`created_at` — the intent instant; the URL dies `PRESIGN_TTL` after it) at the top of the intent endpoint, **the same number as the quarantine bucket's lifecycle ceiling** (two mechanisms, one number); **the mechanism grew a `where` criterion AND a structural guard** — `EPHEMERAL_TABLES` names the four tables whose every row is ephemeral, and `purge_stale` **raises before executing anything** when any other model arrives without a criterion, because `delete(model).where(column < now - grace)` pointed at `media` unchanged would have deleted READY, PUBLISHED photographs silently and totally (**pinned both ways**: the guard, and a stale `ready` photograph with a derivative row surviving the intent endpoint's reap beside a stale `pending_upload` row that does not). The cost, recorded not hidden: an abandoned batch holds its reservation for a day — up to 1.25 GB against a 10 GB free tier; no second state releases it earlier (`failed` is not borrowed); revisit trigger: the first real person who hits a quota wall behind an upload they cancelled. **`uploaded_at` decided — migration 0017, one column**: nullable, no default, **NULL at intent and stamped by confirm** in the transaction that moves the rung (claimed_at's shape) — the rename to `intent_at` lost because `created_at` already IS the intent instant and a second column carrying it is the two-representations defect; the downgrade restores 0016's shape honestly (NULL rows backfilled with `created_at`, which is exactly what 0016's default would have stamped). **The quarantine key layout has one home: `storage.quarantine_key(media_id)`** (`uploads/<id>` — a pure function of the row id, imported by the endpoint and, at CK-35, the worker). **Presigned URLs are never logged, on any path** — the module logs nothing; **pinned by test on the success path AND on a failure after presigning** (the second item's presign made to raise: the request 500s, no row is committed, and the first item's URL and signature appear in no log record, no stream, and no response). `verify_schema.py` → head **0017**, **125 assertions** (5 new: `uploaded_at` nullable + no default; `uploaded_at` set iff the row has left `pending_upload`; every row's size within (0, 25 MB]; every row's content type `image/%` — a prefix, so widening the allowlist within images never fails a deployed verifier while a video type does; `media` joins the informational row counts, where a count above zero is a fact to record), **every one negative-tested** (a temporary downgrade — 3 FAILs incl. the head; scratch rows in the disposable test DB — 3 FAILs; exit 1 each, restored to 125/0). **241 backend tests** (24 new: 21 in `tests/test_media_intents.py` — no network, botocore presigns locally, the HEAD stubbed at one name; 2 in `test_retention.py` — the guard and the criterion; 1 in `test_media_schema.py` — 0017's shape; **prior tests unedited** except the two files' import lines); `alembic check` clean before and after; up/down/up verified in psql (`YES|` → `NO|now()` → `YES|`); frontend untouched. **Dev-deploy: the (ck) deferral comes home** — with a row creatable through the API for the first time, removing a date that has a photograph on it is Steven's check against the deployed app, alongside the first real upload, a refused video, a refused second confirm, and the verifier at 0017/125. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-34-report.md`.
- **1.28.0** (2026-09-07): Phase CK-33 — **the R2 client, and proving the credential split is real** (second phase of the media arc; **infrastructure only** — no endpoint, no router, no `api/` file, no migration: head stays **0016** and `verify_schema.py` stays at **120** assertions; the intent endpoint is CK-34, the worker CK-35). **The deliverable is a test that had never been run**: pipeline record §3 claims the serving credential cannot read quarantine, §11 records the credentials as provisioned on 2026-09-07, and nothing had ever exercised the claim. New **`backend/scripts/verify_r2_credentials.py`** (the `verify_schema.py` mould — a `Checks` tally, PASS/FAIL lines, exit 1 on any failure; prints outcomes, counts and bucket names, **never a credential, a presigned URL, the endpoint, or an object's contents**; failures name the exception class and S3 error code, not the message) uploads a **generated** test object into quarantine through a presigned PUT with the upload credential, proves a body of another length is rejected (§6.6 — the size is signed, not trusted), reads it back with the upload credential (asserted **positively** — R2 has no write-only level, §3's honest bound, so a future scope change is visible), then **asserts the serve credential is REFUSED on that object, with refusal distinguished from every other failure**: a `ClientError` coded `AccessDenied` passes; a network error, a missing bucket, a broken key (`SignatureDoesNotMatch` — a mistyped serve secret is refused everywhere and must not read as a narrow scope), and an unknown 403 code each FAIL with their classification; and the check reports not-meaningful when the PUT itself failed (a refusal on an object never written proves nothing — a wrong bucket name would look identical). It pins the other two edges (upload refused on published; serve refused WRITING to published, a stray write deleted again with the credential that made it), confirms serve reads published (a list, and a presigned GET through the serving path), and deletes the object with the upload credential in a **`finally`**, confirming it gone (the 24-hour lifecycle rule is the backstop, not the plan). **Negative-tested two ways**: 24 committed stub-driven tests (`tests/test_verify_r2_credentials.py`) make every assertion fail the way it should, and a live run against a local MinIO with two policy-scoped users (the as-provisioned split → **10/10, exit 0**) was sabotaged five ways — serve credential swapped for upload (assertion 3 FAILs "READ the quarantine object"), wrong quarantine bucket (the PUT fails and assertion 3 reports not-meaningful), dead endpoint (nine network FAILs), mistyped serve secret (`SignatureDoesNotMatch`, "the credential itself was rejected"), and an all-buckets serve token (reads quarantine AND writes published — both FAIL, the stray object removed, both buckets empty afterwards) — exit 1 each. **The run against live R2 is Steven's, from the Render Web Shell** (WORKING-ON-NOW check (cm)); if assertion 3 fails there, that is a finding about live infrastructure, not a script bug. **The dependency, justified on the record: `botocore==1.43.89`, not `boto3`** — presigning is SigV4 (a canonical request, a scoped credential string, an HMAC chain), the WebAuthn class of thing never hand-rolled; boto3 is a convenience layer over this exact client and what it adds (the resource API, s3transfer's multipart) this arc does not use; one pin instead of two that must move together, three transitive packages instead of five. **`app/services/storage.py` is the one home for R2 access**: `UploadClient` (quarantine, the upload credential) and `ServeClient` (published, the serve credential) are **distinct types each knowing exactly one bucket**; `presign_upload` takes only the former and `presign_read` only the latter (TypeError otherwise), **no module-level default client exists** (pinned by test), application code never names a bucket, and the `.raw` botocore client is reachable for the verifier's deliberate wrong-bucket probes and nothing else. The presigned PUT signs **Content-Length and Content-Type** into the URL (`X-Amz-SignedHeaders`; two intents differing only in size sign differently — pinned); `PRESIGN_TTL` is **one constant (15 minutes)** for both methods; §9's rules live in the module docstring, **the never-log ban sits at the two return sites**, and both result types redact the URL from their repr (a repr lands in a traceback, a traceback in a log). Client config: region `auto`, path-style addressing, SigV4, and **Cloudflare's checksum guidance for botocore ≥ 1.36** (`request_checksum_calculation`/`response_checksum_validation` = `when_required` — the newer defaults add CRC32 trailers R2 does not support). No FastAPI import; **no network in any unit test** — botocore presigns locally, so `tests/test_storage.py` (18 new) asserts on URLs genuinely produced by the signing configuration, not a stub's echo. **Config: all seven R2 settings REQUIRED, no defaults — decided, not accidental**: `email_api_key` may default because `email_mode` gives the empty value a meaning; nothing gates media, so an empty R2 value can only mean misconfigured, and the only choice is where that surfaces — at boot in front of whoever deployed (the `session_secret` precedent, chosen) or at the first upload in front of a relative; required also makes a green deploy prove the dashboard values are present. Bucket names get no default either (a default naming the dev buckets is what a prod service would silently inherit). **Consequence, stated: every local `.env` needs the seven lines from `.env.example`** — real values only when exercising presigning (reuse the dashboard's two tokens; never mint a third), placeholders boot fine otherwise; the test suite supplies its own on a reserved TLD in `conftest.py`. **`render.yaml`: slot declarations that create no values** — the dashboard values were set out-of-band on 2026-09-07 *before* these lines existed (the SESSION_SECRET ordering); the two bucket names are plain `value:`s, the four credential halves `sync: false`, and **`R2_ENDPOINT_URL` is `sync: false` too — one deliberate divergence from the kickoff's "three as plain value"**: the value carries the Cloudflare account id, which no file in either root records, and a placeholder `value:` would overwrite the live value on the next blueprint sync. **`verify_schema.py` provenance rider** (found running CK-32's check (cj) from the Web Shell): the fallback line named `backend/.env` even on Render, where no `.env` exists and `DATABASE_URL` comes from the process environment — it now reports which (`process environment` / `backend/.env`), and the remote-host TLS branch's comment is widened to say it covers Render's internal host too, so the Web Shell connection is encrypted rather than merely "inside Render's network"; **no TLS logic changed**, 120/120 locally with both provenance lines observed. **217 backend tests** (42 new: 18 + 24; the prior 175 unedited except `conftest.py`); `alembic check` clean; frontend untouched. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-33-report.md`.
- **1.27.0** (2026-09-07): Phase CK-32 — **the media schema: the derivative layer shape, the job columns, and the label that must not block a delete** (first phase of the media arc; **schema only** — no R2, no `boto3`, no endpoint, no `render.yaml` or `.env.example` change; the intent endpoint is CK-33, the worker CK-34). **Migration 0016 amends `media`** (it has existed since 0001 with no surface and no rows — verified zero on the dev DB at kickoff): **(1)** `media_status` grows to **`pending_upload | uploaded | processing | ready | failed`** in that database order (`ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS ... BEFORE` — a label cannot be dropped, so the downgrade leaves the three and says so; **and a label added inside a transaction is unusable until it commits**, which is why the migration adds labels and uses none, why `'pending_upload'` could not become the default, and why the suite alone would not catch a violation — a fresh test DB creates the type in the same transaction, the one exemption; recorded in the migration's docstring). **(2)** The four job columns `attempts`/`available_at`/`claimed_at`/`last_error` — the media row IS the job, no jobs table — and **`status` loses its `'processing'` server default**: a writer must state the rung (an omitted status would otherwise be reclaimed by the worker against an object never uploaded). **(3) The shape** (`decisions/2026-09-07-media-derivative-schema-shape.md`, docs root): **`media_derivatives`, one row per layer** (`MediaDerivative`; `media_id` FK `ON DELETE CASCADE`, `layer` enum `media_layer`, `storage_key` UNIQUE, `content_type`, `size_bytes`, `storage_class` no default, `last_accessed`; `UNIQUE (media_id, layer)`), carrying **NO `publication_state`, `removed_at`, `status`, or lifecycle column — ever** (media-layers §8, made structural: the verifier asserts the absence). The object columns leave `media`: `storage_key`/`storage_class`/`last_accessed` **dropped** (the quarantine original's key is derived from `media.id`, never stored), `size_bytes`/`content_type` **renamed `upload_size_bytes` (NOT NULL) / `upload_content_type`** — facts about the upload, one meaning at every rung. **(4) `media.occurrence_id` → `ON DELETE SET NULL`** (the 0015 drop-and-recreate pattern): the CK-30 dormant 500, closed for media — a label, never an owner; the photograph survives and loses its label; **the handler is untouched** (`gatherings.py` has no diff — not a third refusal); `posts` identical and deliberately untouched. `verify_schema.py` → head **0016**, **120 assertions** (38 net new over CK-30's 82; every one negative-tested — 36 in one break-everything run, `ready` via `RENAME VALUE`, the derivative-integrity check via scratch rows, a temporary downgrade for the rest; exit 1 each, all reverted). **175 backend tests** (6 new, `tests/test_media_schema.py`: the ladder in the DB matches the model label for label — Alembic never diffs enum labels; no default on `status`; the job columns' defaults; the object/state split read from `information_schema`; one row per layer, one object per row, layers die with their photograph; **deleting a date keeps its photographs and clears only the label, on both the plain and the confirmed path**; `test_schema_keeper.py`'s three `Media(...)` constructions edited only where they named the retired columns); frontend untouched (no diff, so build and lint were not re-run); up/down/up genuinely restores 0015 (columns, nullability, the `'processing'` default, the table and layer type, the FK rule `n`→`a`→`n` — verified in psql); `alembic check` clean before and after.
- **1.26.0** (2026-09-06): Phase CK-30 — **two consequences of naming people: companions out of ATTENDEES, and a date that can be removed** (both found verifying CK-29, and neither a defect in what it shipped: the roster changed from integers to people's names, and two things written for integers were never re-examined). **(1) `ATTENDEES` suppresses companion names** (decided 2026-09-06). The gate admits any caller whose own response is `yes` — written when the roster held counts; after CK-29 it let every attendee enumerate every other attendee's companions, **names, children's first names among them: a second-order disclosure nobody opted into**. Under ATTENDEES a non-host caller's roster rows now carry **`companions: null`** — null, never `[]`, which would claim "brought nobody" about a row whose names are merely withheld — while **the totals stay** ("three going" is the information the mode exists to give; suppressing the total as well would break the mode rather than narrow it). `HOST_ONLY` and `INVITEES` unchanged; **the host's floor unchanged** (full list, names included, in every mode); **`own` deliberately ungated in every mode** — the caller typed those names. Frontend renders **", bringing N"** from the row's server-computed `total` where names are withheld, so the party size survives the narrowing. **(2) `DELETE /occurrences/{id}` — refuse once, then obey** (it was a live 500: a bare `db.delete` against an FK with no `ondelete`, reachable through CK-18's per-date remove control since CK-27 first gave `rsvps` rows a way to exist — and refusing outright would leave a host no way to remove a date short of deleting the gathering, while cascading silently would destroy answers CK-27 deliberately preserved by shipping no RSVP delete endpoint). **Migration 0015, one change**: `rsvps.occurrence_id` gains **`ON DELETE CASCADE`** (matching `rsvp_companions`' 0014 shape — an RSVP cannot outlive its date, and the schema says so rather than leaving a handler to remember; companions cascade transitively; the down-migration restores the no-`ondelete` FK under the same conventional name). The endpoint: a date somebody answered refuses **once** — a **409** carrying the stable marker **`confirmation_required`** and `rsvp_count`, the number of answers that would be discarded — and the repeat with **`?confirm=true`** proceeds. **The two refusals are machine-distinguishable, not merely differently worded**: the last-occurrence rule keeps its 422 field-error shape exactly, is **never confirmable**, and **runs first**, before anything reads the flag — pinned by a date that is both the last one AND answered drawing the 422 with and without `confirm=true`. A date with **no** answers deletes as it always has — the warning appears only when there is something to warn about. Frontend: the remove control switches on **status + marker, never wording**; the warning states the human consequence in people, not rows ("3 people have answered for this date. Removing it discards their answers."); removal is a deliberate second click (**"Remove it anyway"**, beside a do-nothing "Don't remove it"), and **the word "cancel" appears nowhere** — cancellation is an unbuilt product concept this phase must not pre-commit; the 422 path stays on `lib/formErrors.ts`, no second mapper. **Every other FK pointing at `occurrences` enumerated and deliberately left alone** (`attendance_records`/`item_slots`/`item_claims` NOT NULL, `posts`/`media` nullable — all no-`ondelete`, all latent 500s with no surface; the right behaviour per table is a separate decision — a photo attached to a removed date may deserve to survive it, unlike an RSVP to it; recorded in database-schema): fix nothing this phase does not need. `verify_schema.py` → head **0015**, **82 assertions** (2 new over CK-29's 80: the FK's target and its delete rule, read from `pg_constraint` the 0014 way), negative-tested via temporary downgrade (2 FAILs + exit 1, reverted). **169 backend tests** (3 new in `test_rsvps.py`: the ATTENDEES matrix with the host floor and `own` in all three modes; refuse-once-then-obey with the RSVPs and companions observed genuinely gone and a sibling date's answer surviving; the last-occurrence refusal ignoring the flag on a date that is both last and answered), **69 frontend** (2 + 1 new); prior tests unedited; up/down/up genuinely restores 0014 (delete rule back to NO ACTION, same name and target, verified in psql); `alembic check` clean; build + lint clean.
- **1.25.0** (2026-09-04): Phase CK-29 — **RSVP is for yourself: named companions replace the head count** (and the principle behind it, now a Hub convention: **store who, compute how many**). `rsvps.adult_count`/`child_count` came from migration 0001, when this was a generic event tool — nobody chose them for CoveyKeep, they survived — and they failed on their own terms before any naming argument: CK-27's deployed verification found a **declined** RSVP carrying `adult_count=2, child_count=1` ("two adults and one child are not coming" is not information; it is noise the pre-populated form manufactured and the row preserved). A family wants to know WHO is coming; the host still gets "twelve coming" for the catering, **derived from named people, never typed** — a typed count can disagree with the list of names beside it, a computed one cannot. **Migration 0014, three changes, one table's scope**: **(a) the two count columns DROPPED** — alpha data is disposable (private-alpha record §1 condition 2), the values are lost and that loss is the recorded price of doing this while the surface is three days old; the down-migration restores 0001's types and defaults, never the values. **(b) `rsvp_companions`** — one row per accompanying person, keyed to the RSVP, ordered (`position`, unique with `rsvp_id`), holding a **name and nothing else**, `ON DELETE CASCADE` with its RSVP. **Deliberately NOT named guest-anything** (the terminology record's §8, the trap this phase was built around): `guest_name`/`guest_email` already mean a person participating with NO account — a different axis, untouched, still NULL on every row — and reusing the word would recreate the `is_observer` four-meanings failure; the check-4 grep confirms zero phase-added "guest" occurrences. **(c) `rsvps.updated_at`** — the row became mutable at CK-27 (the upsert is the point) with only a `created_at`, and that gap cost CK-28's check (bt) its evidence (rows that didn't match expectation could be read but not accounted for); the 0004/0010 shape exactly — nullable, NULL until first changed, **stamped by the upsert's update path only**, so it never merely mirrors `created_at` (pinned by test: first write leaves it NULL, a changed answer stamps it, `created_at` doesn't move). **Companions are names, not people**: no `person_id`, no invitation, no notification, and **no authorization decision anywhere consults them** — the `stay_included` discipline, pinned the same way (a write creates no `people` or `gathering_invitations` row, buys no mutation rights). **No adult/child flag** — that is the head count coming back in a smaller coat; whether a host ever needs to know a companion is a child is recorded as an open question needing a decision record, not answered in passing. `PUT /occurrences/{id}/rsvp`: counts gone from body and response; optional ordered `companions` list **replacing the RSVP's companions wholesale on every write** (bounded at 10, names trimmed + blank-rejected per CK-20 with item-level 422s landing on the offending entry); **companions accompany a "yes"/"maybe" only** — a "no" with companions draws the field-level 422 (`stay_included`'s shape; the declined-row noise refused at the door), and an empty list on a "no" is the clearing path. `GET .../rsvps` rows carry companion names and a **`total` computed at read time** (the row's person + companions; never stored, never accepted from a client); upsert semantics, the no-delete rule, the audience, and the visibility enum all unchanged. **DATA-HANDLING: names disclose MORE than counts** (children's first names among them, visible to whoever the host's setting admits), so `rsvp_list_visibility` and its INVITEES default carry more weight, not less — companion names are third-party data an attendee declared, bounded, deleted with their RSVP, never resolved to a person. Frontend: the two number inputs replaced by **"Bringing anyone?"** — an add/remove list of name rows, **empty by default (adding nobody costs no clicks; an added-but-empty row is omitted from the send and counts as no change)**, item errors landing on their row via sent-index mapping, roster rendering each person's companions beneath them, the host seeing "Total going: N — counted from the names" summed over the Going rows (`lib/rsvps.ts::totalGoing`). `verify_schema.py` → head **0014**, **80 assertions** (17 new over CK-28's 63: the drop both columns, `updated_at` shape, the table + its NOT NULLs, **no identity columns on companions** (`person_id`/`account_id`/`email`/`destination` absent), FK-to-rsvps + CASCADE via `pg_constraint`, and three integrity checks — no blank name, bound ≤ 10, none on a 'no'), **negative-tested both ways**: temporary downgrade (10 FAILs, reverted) and a scratch blank-name-on-a-"no" row in the disposable test DB (2 FAILs + exit 1; removing the scratch RSVP took its companion with it — the cascade observed live). **166 backend tests** (4 new in `test_rsvps.py`; the counts test rewritten as the companions test; the fixture and two roster/upsert tests edited only where they asserted the removed fields), **66 frontend** (2 new component tests + a `totalGoing` pin; fixtures and body assertions updated for the new shape); up/down/up genuinely restores 0013 (columns, types, defaults verified in psql); `alembic check` clean; build + lint clean.
- **1.24.0** (2026-09-03): Phase CK-28 — **rename: the gathering's admin is the host, before there are two** (`decisions/2026-09-03-co-hosts.md` §5). The product says host, the schema said admin, and the two words have had exactly one referent — but the co-host design adds a second party who administers a gathering, at which point an unqualified "admin" means two things: the `is_observer` failure over again, which CK-27 spent a migration renaming out of the schema.
