This phase pays the same debt **before** it is incurred, mechanically and alone — no co-host table, no `may_administer()`, **no behaviour change of any kind** (every gate decides exactly what it decided before). **Migration 0013, one rename**: `gatherings.admin_account_id` → `gatherings.host_account_id` via `op.alter_column(..., new_column_name=...)` (0012's operation), plus the FK constraint renamed `fk_gatherings_admin_account_id` → `fk_gatherings_host_account_id` in the same migration — the **0009 precedent** (`RENAME CONSTRAINT` preserves the constraint rather than re-creating it; the metadata naming convention derives FK names from column names, so leaving the old name would strand the DB out of step with the models, and `test_keeping`'s constraint-name assertions pin it). Nullability, the FK target, and every behaviour behind the column are untouched: **NULL host is still the claimable state** (CK-13), unkeep and account deletion still relinquish it, creation still makes the creator first keeper + host in one transaction. **The API response field renames with the column** (`host_account_id` in every gathering body — backend and frontend spell the identifier identically, so this is one rename, not two), and the derived booleans follow: `is_admin` → `is_host` (`rsvps.py`), `isAdmin` → `isHost` (`GatheringDetail.tsx`). **`groups.admin_person_id`/`backup_admin_person_id` deliberately untouched** — a group has an admin, a gathering has a host; the words differing is the point. The kickoff's 52 occurrences across 20 files confirmed exactly and all renamed; the only `admin_account_id` remaining under `backend/`+`frontend/` is migration history (0009 creates the column, 0013 renames it — the CK-27 precedent: 0012 still names `is_observer`). Helper names `_gathering_for_admin`/`_occurrence_for_admin` deliberately keep their names — the co-host phase's audit classifies every check into `is_host()` (reserved) vs `may_administer()` (delegable), and renaming the helpers now would pre-empt that classification. `verify_schema.py` → head **0013**, still **63 assertions** (no new invariant; the existing column assertion renamed with its subject). **162 backend / 63 frontend tests green with no logic edits** (identifier-only changes throughout); up/down/up genuinely restores 0012 (column name AND FK name both revert, verified in psql); `alembic check` clean; build + lint clean.
- **1.23.0** (2026-09-02): Phase CK-27 — **RSVP: the "are you coming" half, and the host's first real option** (`rsvps` had existed since 0001, re-pointed to the occurrence at 0008, with no surface; this kickoff superseded an earlier draft — `decisions/2026-09-02-participation-terminology.md` changed what two columns mean). **Migration 0012, two changes, no new table**: **(a) `rsvps.is_observer` → `rsvps.stay_included`** — "observer" is retired from the product vocabulary entirely (the word was carrying four jobs), and the column's settled meaning is **"I can't make it, but keep me included"**: the third RSVP answer, stored as a modifier on a "no" (the API refuses it with any other response — a stored meaningless value is a second representation waiting to disagree). **It changes INTEREST, never access: no authorization check anywhere may consult it** — pinned by test (`test_stay_included_never_affects_access` asserts two "no"-answering invitees, one flagged, hold byte-identical read surfaces), and by a verifier assertion. Closes an ambiguity that had stood since 0001 with an empty notes cell. **(b) `gatherings.rsvp_list_visibility`** — enum `HOST_ONLY | INVITEES | ATTENDEES`, **default `INVITEES`** (adult/child counts disclose household composition, so the default is not broader; type-derived defaults belong to the presets work). `requires_approval`'s shape exactly: a value on the gathering, **set explicitly in application code on every create** — the server default exists for 0012's backfill of pre-existing rows and is never relied on. New router **`app/api/rsvps.py`**: **`PUT /occurrences/{id}/rsvp`** (upsert the caller's own RSVP — the partial unique `uq_rsvps_occurrence_person` makes changing your mind the normal case, IntegrityError-backstopped for the create race; body: response `yes|no|maybe`, `stay_included`, `adult_count`/`child_count` default 1/0 and **bounded 0–99** — unbounded is a SmallInteger overflow surfacing as a 500 — and optional `arrival_time`) and **`GET /occurrences/{id}/rsvps`** (the list). **Authorization is CK-25's audience inherited exactly** (keeper OR admin OR invitee; a stranger's 404 is byte-identical to a missing id on both endpoints — nobody learns an occurrence exists by RSVPing to it). The list is filtered by the visibility setting, with two rules holding in **every** mode: **the admin always sees the full list** (a narrower setting must never show the host less than HOST_ONLY does — the one judgment call this phase added to the kickoff's letter, recorded in the api-reference) and **the caller always sees their own RSVP** via `own` (never locked out of their own answer; unanswered is `null`, never a defaulted "no"). Roster rows carry **display names and counts only — an email address appears in no RSVP response body** (the CK-25 rule with more force: more people read this list). **No delete endpoint, deliberately**: changing your mind is `response: "no"`, which keeps the record that they answered — a withdrawn row loses information the host needs and creates a second representation of "not coming" (the CK-13/CK-20 defect class; stated in the api-reference so nobody adds one as a convenience). **`arrival_time` is a bare `time` and the CK-17 conversion is never applied to it** — no date, no zone, a clock time on the occurrence's own day; an offset-carrying value draws a field-level 422, the server echoes exactly the wall clock sent, and the frontend sends the input value verbatim (pinned three ways: backend round trip, `lib/rsvps.test.ts` under a zone forced to differ from the runner's, and a component-boundary body assertion under a differing profile zone — the same trap as CK-17/CK-18 with the **opposite** fix, which is why `lib/rsvps.ts` takes no zone anywhere and formats via UTC-pinned Intl). **Guest columns stay NULL** on every row this phase touches (guest RSVPs are their own later phase; verifier-asserted). `GatheringPatch` grows `rsvp_list_visibility` (admin-only like the rest, explicit null refused — the list always has SOME visibility). Frontend: **collapsed-by-default per-occurrence RSVP block** on the detail (the CK-25 invitations pattern — the page stays one request and a many-date season can't reproduce the CK-17 N+1 on load): the three responses in the terminology record's plain words (Going / Maybe / Can't make it), **keep-me-included surfacing only when the answer is "no"** (meaningless otherwise; sent `false` with any other answer), counts and arrival time on going/maybe, dirty-tracked save, roster per visibility with an honest hint when the caller can't see it; the admin's edit form gains the **visibility selector** riding the same PATCH; `lib/formErrors.ts` maps everything (no second 422 mapper). `verify_schema.py` → head **0012**, **63 assertions** (rename both sides, the NOT NULL setting, no account-holder row with guest identity, `stay_included` on a `'no'` only), **negative-tested both ways**: temporary downgrade (4 FAILs + exit 1, reverted) and a scratch violating row in the disposable test DB (2 FAILs + exit 1, removed). **162 backend tests** (13 new, `tests/test_rsvps.py`), **63 frontend** (9 new: `lib/rsvps.test.ts` + `routes/rsvps.test.tsx`); prior tests unedited except the two `detailBody` fixture factories gaining the new response-body field (a shape change, not a behavior change); up/down/up genuinely restores 0011 (column name back, enum and setting gone, verified); `alembic check` clean; build + lint clean.
- **1.22.0** (2026-09-02): Phase CK-26 — **the private-alpha notice: real text behind `/tos`, and the ToS version number settled** (condition 4 of `decisions/2026-08-31-private-alpha-scope.md` §1 — the alpha was feature-complete at CK-25 and blocked on this page; onboarding family is now unblocked). **`routes/Tos.tsx` rewritten** from the CK-6 placeholder into a plain private-test notice that states, in order: private test by personal invitation, not a service, don't rely on it; **the data will not be kept** (dev database, deleted when the test ends, nothing carries over — keep a copy of anything that matters); what is stored (email address, display name, timezone, gatherings created or invited to, routine sign-in records — and **photo upload stated as not existing** rather than implied); **the console-mode disclosure** — sign-in links print to a server log the operator can read (`EMAIL_MODE=console`; the line most tempting to drop for sounding awkward, and the one that makes the notice true rather than boilerplate); real terms + privacy policy coming and **will require acceptance** (queue item 0b's first genuine user); how to reach Steven and how to stop (tell him, or delete the account in Settings). The heading says what the page is (`{PRODUCT_NAME} is a private test`); the route stays `/tos` (renaming is churn); it never calls itself a Terms of Service or claims compliance, durability, or security. **`SignIn.tsx`'s checkbox label reworded** — "I've read the private test notice and agree to take part" — because "I agree to the Terms of Service" became false the moment the page stopped being terms; the **mechanism is untouched** (affirmative, unchecked by default, submit disabled until checked — contract-formation hygiene). **Version numbering settled: the alpha notice IS version 1; the public terms (with counsel in the Hub's `legal-drafts/`, ToS 0.4.0 / privacy 0.6.0) publish as version 2.** No constant change, no validator change, no backfill: `TOS_VERSION = 1` stands in `backend/app/api/auth.py` AND `frontend/src/lib/api.ts`, the validator's `1 <= value <= TOS_VERSION` bound already rejects 0, and the twelve existing acceptance rows become **retroactively true** — the notice describes precisely what those people were doing — instead of needing a live-data backfill. Do not "tidy" this by renumbering. **No `/privacy` route, deliberately** (review-pack decision #12 stays open for the public terms; the notice covers what is collected in one page). **54 frontend tests** (4 new, `routes/tos.test.tsx`: the load-bearing statements pinned; the checkbox label + unchecked default + disabled-until-checked mechanism; **`TOS_VERSION === 1` as a guard** so a later tidy-up cannot silently renumber; the product name flowing from `brand.ts` — the Tos source may contain no spelling of the name, the brand.test.tsx discipline); prior tests unedited. Backend untouched (149 tests green, no `backend/` diff); build + lint clean.
- **1.21.0** (2026-09-02): Phase CK-25 — **person-targeted invitations**: pending invites, acceptance, and the first multiplayer surface (`gathering_invitations` had existed since 0008 with no surface; this is the gate on the private family alpha). **Migration 0011**: `gathering_invitations_pending` — **channel-agnostic from the first row** (`channel` EMAIL|SMS + `destination`, never an `email` column; launch-shape decision 2026-08-31: the primary invitation channel at launch is a text message, so enabling SMS is a validator-and-delivery change, never a migration against live rows — **this phase delivers EMAIL only and refuses SMS at the endpoint** with a usable field-level 422, delivery blocked upstream by A2P and therefore the name gate); SHA-256 token hash only, 7-day `expires_at`, `consumed_at`, **`revoked_at`** (one deliberate addition over the kickoff's column list — see the rate-limit sentence), NOT NULL `invited_by_person_id` provenance, partial unique on (gathering, channel, destination) WHERE unconsumed-and-unrevoked, CHECK `consumed_or_revoked` (never both). **The pending table is the design**: the invitee may not exist, and the `gathering_invitations` row is created ONLY at acceptance — pre-creating a `people` row would leave permanent rows holding an address a third party supplied, for invitees who never accept: the CK-24 defect class reintroduced one phase later, rejected. New router **`app/api/invitations.py`**: `POST`/`GET /gatherings/{id}/invitations` (admin only, 404-not-403 byte-identical to a missing id), `DELETE /invitations/pending/{id}` (revoke), unauthenticated `POST /invitations/preview`, `POST /invitations/accept`. **Rate-limited on the `request_link` pattern** (10/gathering, 20/inviter over `RATE_WINDOW`), which is exactly why `revoked_at` exists: **revocation and supersession STAMP the row and force `expires_at` to now, never delete** — pending rows ARE the tally, counted by `created_at`, and a tally the caller can delete rows out of is a disabled rate limit (the CK-24 lesson applied forward); the reaper (`purge_stale`, its **fourth customer**, called at the top of the create endpoint) removes stamped rows an hour past the forced expiry. **Acceptance routes through the session, never around `/auth/verify`**: ONE token, redeemed post-sign-in — the emailed link targets the frontend (`${APP_BASE_URL}/invitations/accept#token=…`, fragment discipline), a signed-out invitee gets an unauthenticated preview (distinguished dead-token screens BEFORE a pointless sign-in) and signs in through the unmodified magic-link path — person, `accounts` row, and `tos_acceptances` row all born in `/auth/verify`, the trap this phase is built around — while the frontend parks the token in localStorage across the inbox round trip (`AuthCallback` routes back when one is parked); `POST /invitations/accept` then consumes the pending row and creates the invitation row in one transaction. **The token is the credential** (the CK-9 email-change precedent): whoever holds the link may accept under any account they sign in with — acceptance is deliberately not bound to the invited address (an SMS destination has no address at all). Idempotent: re-accept → `already_accepted` + the gathering id, never a 500 (the partial person-target unique is checked first and IntegrityError-backstopped); dead tokens distinguished (`expired`/`used`/`revoked`/`invalid`). **Reads widen for the first time**: `_gathering_for_read` and `GET /gatherings` are now kept-OR-person-invited-OR-admin (the list via two EXISTS in the same ONE statement — the CK-20 statement-count pin passes unedited); an invitee reads the same detail body a keeper reads, sees the gathering on their own list (without that, the emailed link would be their only way back), and gets 404 on every mutation and on the invitation roster — accepted entries show **display names only**, an account email is never auto-exposed to an admin, and creation never looks the destination up in `people` at all (the CK-5 non-enumeration rule). **`requires_approval` deliberately untouched**: the recorded relaxation is a GROUP-TYPE rule (ON for TEAM/CONGREGATION-sourced, OFF for HOUSEHOLD/CLUB) and no group-targeted invitations exist, so a person-targeted invite has no inviting context to default from — still hard-coded `true`, trigger undischarged, stated in the router docstring and api-reference so nobody reads it as missed. Frontend: `/invitations/accept` landing screen (deliberately NOT behind `RequireAuth`; auto-accepts when signed in and lands the invitee on the gathering), `GatheringDetail` grows a **collapsed-by-default** admin Invitations section (email-only form — no SMS control the API would refuse, the CK-18 lesson; pending list with per-row revoke and expiry; accepted display names). `verify_schema.py` → head **0011**, **58 assertions** (table shape, both nullability sides, the CHECK, `email`/`person_id` absent from the pending table, normalized-destination integrity; **no row-count assertion** — a reaped table, the CK-24 rule), negative-tested via temporary downgrade (5 FAILs + exit 1, reverted). **149 backend tests** (17 new, `tests/test_invitations.py` — accept-as-new-person asserts person + `accounts` + `tos_acceptances` all exist afterwards, the silent-failure regression test), **50 frontend** (10 new, `routes/invitations.test.tsx`); prior tests unedited; up/down/up genuinely restores 0010 (table and enum gone, verified); `alembic check` clean; build + lint clean.
- **1.20.0** (2026-08-30): Phase CK-24 — the **ephemeral-data reaper**: `magic_link_tokens` and `email_change_requests` are finally purged (found 2026-08-29 while drafting the privacy copy — counsel review pack Finding 1: every sign-in ever requested persisted as an address in the clear bound to an IP, and every email-change request as a `new_email`, including the rows deliberately written for an already-taken address, i.e. third parties' addresses supplied by people who do not control them). **Built as a mechanism, not a one-off**: new **`app/services/retention.py::purge_stale(db, model, column, now, *, grace=PURGE_GRACE)`**, generic over model and timestamp column, returning a row count and nothing else; three live customers (magic-link rows at the top of `auth.request_link`, email-change rows at the top of `profile.request_email_change`, and `passkeys.py`'s pre-existing `_purge_stale_challenges` **migrated onto it** — deleted, both begin endpoints call the shared helper, the passkey tests unedited) and two recorded future ones (dietary requests after their occurrence passes, guest→host RSVP notes with their contact details — neither built; `column` is a parameter because they will key off an occurrence date). **The one constraint the phase turns on: the purge keys off `expires_at`, NEVER `consumed_at`, and `PURGE_GRACE` (1 hour) is strictly greater than `RATE_WINDOW` (15 minutes).** The rate limits count rows by `created_at` over the window regardless of consumption or supersession (a new request forces the previous row's `expires_at` to now — no inbox access needed) — purge sooner and a caller who consumes or supersedes each token never accumulates a tally; the review pack's own "delete consumed rows immediately" advice would have been exactly this regression, and is corrected on the record there. Arithmetic: a magic-link row lives ~1h15m from creation, an email-change row ~2h, a superseded row one hour past its forced expiry — every one outlives the window. `tests/test_retention.py` (10 new → **132 backend tests**): `PURGE_GRACE > RATE_WINDOW` asserted directly; the limit still trips across a purge (five rows, four superseded, purge removes zero, sixth is 429); the zero-grace counter-example removes the four; consumed-but-recent survives; the superseded email-change row and the third-party-address row go after the grace, never before; one wiring test per customer. **Opportunistic, deliberately** — rides the write transaction each request already opens; no cron, worker, or Render service, so rows linger on an idle deployment until the next request, which is why **no `verify_schema.py` assertion was added** (it would flap on an idle DB for a legitimate reason). Never logs, echoes, or counts-by-value what it removes. **No migration** (`alembic check` clean, `alembic/versions/` unchanged); `sessions`, `tos_acceptances`, `webauthn_credentials`, every content table, and `delete_account` untouched; frontend untouched. Discharges the CK-5.1 deploy.md trigger ("revisit the retention window when the cleanup job is written") and closes review-pack Finding 1; the privacy draft's placeholder filled and its two apology paragraphs deleted **in the same phase** — copy describing a defect that no longer exists is the same defect class in the other direction.
- **1.19.0** (2026-08-27): Phase CK-22 — clearing an optional field: **explicit `null` means remove** (JSON Merge Patch semantics on the gatherings router; `decisions/2026-08-27-optional-field-clearing.md`). CK-20's recorded trigger fired 2026-08-27 — Steven needed to remove a location within minutes of using the deployed feature — so the open question is answered: **an absent field means "leave alone"; an explicit `null` means "clear."** Pydantic hands both to the model as `None`, so the distinction comes from **`model_fields_set`** (the set of fields actually present in the request body), never a custom sentinel. **The clearable set is exactly three fields**: `OccurrencePatch.ends_at`/`.location`/`.map_url` (all already nullable — **no migration**; `alembic check` clean, no new file). Not clearable, each refused with a field-level 422: `starts_at` and `title` (NOT NULL columns) and **`memorial_decedent_name`** (the memorial CHECK requires it present iff the type is memorial — clearing it on a memorial would violate the constraint, and it is already NULL everywhere else; refused at the model, never an IntegrityError surfacing as a 500). **Blank stays rejected**: `""` is never a clear — letting blank also clear would reintroduce the second representation of "no value" CK-20 banned (the CK-13 defect class); a blank still draws its field-level 422, and CK-21's scheme allowlist runs unchanged on every non-null value. **Both `_something_to_patch` validators rewritten against `model_fields_set`** — a patch is empty when no field was *provided*, not when every value is None: `{"location": null}` has all-None values, and the old all-values-None test would have silently rejected every explicit-null clear as "empty" (pinned by test on both models). Endpoints apply fields by presence (`"x" in body.model_fields_set`). **Frontend: blanking a field clears it** — no separate Remove control: dirty-tracking treats "had a saved value, now blank" as a real change, a blanked field goes out as explicit `null`, an already-empty field stays **omitted** (a null for an already-null field is a no-op write that muddies the contract), and a whitespace-only entry goes out as typed so the server's blank-rejection 422 renders. The CK-18 helper text replaced ("Leaving the end time, location, or map link blank removes what's saved there" — the corrected-not-removed wording became false the moment this shipped); the decedent hint ("can be corrected, not removed") stays true and stays. **No new verifier assertion, deliberately**: CK-20's no-empty-string assertion now also guards the clearing path — a clearing bug that wrote `""` instead of NULL would fail an assertion that already exists. Local verifier 46/46 at head **0010**. 122 backend tests (2 new: the absent/null/blank three-way distinction pinned on both patch models), 40 frontend (4 new); prior tests unedited; build + lint clean. `/me/profile` deliberately NOT extended — its own fields and history (the CK-7 silent-timezone-capture rule in particular: clearing `timezone` would re-arm the capture, a design decision of its own); recorded as a finding with its home in WORKING-ON-NOW's Backlog.
- **1.18.0** (2026-08-27): Phase CK-21 — user-controlled URLs: `occurrences.map_url` scheme-allowlisted server-side, guarded at render, and **the XSS bound corrected** (the part that outlives the fix). `map_url` is user-supplied text rendered as a link href (`GatheringDetail.tsx`) and nothing validated its scheme — a stored `javascript:` value executes on click. **Self-XSS today** (only the admin writes it; only keepers/admin read it — the same person), but the window opens with invitations, and the session JWT lives in `localStorage` — exactly the cost `decisions/2026-08-20-browser-session-storage.md` §2 priced on the ground that nothing renders user-generated HTML. The ground was intact; the bound was drawn around the wrong thing: **a `javascript:` href is XSS with no HTML injection anywhere**, which is why CK-19's `innerHTML` grep verified the bound and could not have seen this. **(1) Backend:** `_clean_map_url` accepts `http`/`https` only — the scheme is **parsed** (`urlsplit`), never prefix-matched (`java\tscript:` is why); embedded tab/CR/LF rejected outright; a netloc required, so the accepted set literally matches the message ("a map link must start with http:// or https://"); **the message never echoes the rejected value** (a reflecting rejection is itself an injection surface) and no map link is ever logged (a location URL can identify a home). One shared helper covers `OccurrenceIn` AND `OccurrencePatch` — create, add, and patch all pinned with `javascript:`, `data:`, `vbscript:`, scheme-relative `//evil.example`, and `java\tscript:`. **(2) Frontend:** new **`lib/url.ts::safeHttpUrl`** — the ONE home for the render guard; a stored value failing it renders as **plain text, visible but never navigable** (a silently vanished field is its own bug). **`target="_blank"` is NOT a security control**: Chrome's observed refusal of `javascript:` URLs in a new tab (deployed app, 2026-08-27) is incidental, browser-dependent, and one same-tab refactor from vanishing — the allowlist and the guard are the reason the sink is safe. **(3) Sink sweep:** every navigation sink in `frontend/src` enumerated (recorded in components.md 1.9.0) — `map_url` confirmed the only user-controlled value reaching one; every other `href`/`Navigate`/`navigate` target is a static literal or a server-minted UUID inside an internal router path; zero HTML sinks exist. **The verification for this class is a sink enumeration, not a grep for `innerHTML`** — now a docs-root CLAUDE.md convention. `verify_schema.py` → **46 assertions** (new: no occurrence stores a non-http(s) `map_url`; detail prints the count only), head still **0010** — **no migration** (`alembic check` clean, no new file); negative-tested via a scratch `javascript:` row in the disposable test DB (FAIL + exit 1, reverted). Local data check: zero occurrences in the dev DB; **the deployed dev DB deliberately holds one `javascript:alert(1)` row** (Steven's 2026-08-27 exploit confirmation, left in place as the assertion's real subject — the deployed verifier fails on it until Steven corrects the row through the UI, then reads 46/46). 120 backend tests (2 new), 36 frontend (4 new); prior tests unedited; build + lint clean.
- **1.17.0** (2026-08-27): Phase CK-20 — backend cleanup: the three findings CK-17/CK-18 reported rather than fixed, **no migration** (verified: every change is response-body or request-validation on existing nullable `Text` columns; `alembic check` clean, no new file under `alembic/versions/`). **(1) `GET /gatherings` items carry an occurrence summary**: `next_occurrence` (`{id, starts_at}` — the earliest occurrence at or after now, else the latest past one; **the next-or-most-recent rule now lives in the API** and is documented in the api-reference, so no consumer re-derives it) and `occurrence_count`; null `next_occurrence` is defensive only (creation requires an occurrence, the last is undeletable). **ONE query, deliberately** — a `DISTINCT ON (gathering_id)` subquery with a window count joined to the kept gatherings; **pinned by a statement-count test** (`test_list_statement_count_does_not_scale_with_gatherings`: equal statement counts at 1 and 4 gatherings, exactly one statement reading `FROM gatherings`) — moving the client's N+1 into a server-side loop would not have been a fix. The list gets its own item builder (`_list_item`); the detail body is untouched. **Frontend workaround deleted**: `Gatherings.tsx` makes one request, the per-row detail fetches and their per-row degrade are gone, and `nextOrMostRecent` is removed from `lib/gatherings.ts` (the rule's one home is now the backend). **(2) `/auth/me` carries `account_id`** — the caller's own id, disclosing nothing about anyone else; `Person` carries it, and `GatheringDetail`'s edit gate is now the **real comparison** `admin_account_id === person.account_id` (CK-18's interim `admin_account_id !== null` replaced; a failed profile load gates closed; the case the old gate could not express — someone *else's* admin — is pinned by test). The equivalence-until-now argument and the discharged widen-the-audience trigger are recorded in the api-reference. **(3) occurrence `location`/`map_url` are trimmed and blank-rejected** on `OccurrenceIn` **and** `OccurrencePatch` (`_clean_location`/`_clean_map_url` mirror `_clean_title`; caps 200 and 2000 — URLs run longer than names), so **NULL stays the one representation of "no value"** (the CK-13 discipline). Honest consequence, recorded not smuggled: with blanks rejected and null meaning "not provided," an optional field can be **corrected but never cleared** — clearing support deliberately NOT added; open question in the api-reference with its trigger (the first person who needs to remove a location rather than change it). `verify_schema.py` → **45 assertions** (new: no occurrence stores an empty-string `location`/`map_url` — vacuous today, same class as the kept-and-in-grace check), head still **0010**; negative-tested via a scratch blank-location row in the disposable test DB (FAIL + exit 1, reverted). Local data check: zero blank rows (the dev DB holds no occurrences). 118 backend tests (4 new; prior 114 green and unedited except `test_gatherings.py`'s import line, which the query-count test mandates); 32 frontend tests (2 new); build + lint clean.
- **1.16.0** (2026-08-26): Phase CK-18 — inline editing on `/gatherings/:id` (the "change" half CK-17 deliberately left out; **no new route** — the read-only detail became the view-and-edit view). The gathering edit form patches **title + decedent name only** (the decedent field rendered only on a memorial, with its constraint labeled — the type is not editable: offering a control the API rejects is worse than none); per-date controls edit dates/location/map link, add a date, remove a date. **Admin-gating and its reported gap**: edit affordances render only for the admin (a non-admin gets CK-17's read-only page byte-for-byte — no edit control exists to probe, so the 404-not-403 posture survives editing), but **the API exposes no way to compare** — `/auth/me` carries the person id, never `account_id` — so the gate is `admin_account_id !== null`, which is provably exact under the shipped surface (creation is the only path to a kept row or admin; the NULL-admin claimable state has no readers) and **must be replaced before any phase widens the read audience** (reported as a backend gap, not fixed — no backend source changed; homed on WORKING-ON-NOW's small-backend-phase item). **The load-edit-save round trip is pinned first** (`lib/datetime.test.ts`): instant → `instantToWallClock` → `wallClockToInstant` three times over, byte-identical, in a zone forced to differ from the runner's and in a DST zone — a zone bug here doesn't fail, it walks the time by the offset per save. **Dirty-tracking**: save is disabled until something changes (a no-op edit sends no PATCH at all), and the backend's "nothing to update" 422 still renders if forced — a **no-op guard** may be pre-empted client-side; a **domain rule** (the memorial gate) never is. **`null` means "not provided"** on every PATCH (the `/me/profile` convention): nothing is clearable, so a blanked optional field is left out of the patch and the constraint is labeled plainly ("can be corrected, not removed") — never a control that fake-clears; blanked *validated* text (title, decedent) goes out as `""` so the server's own refusal renders. **Season cap** pinned on all three `loc` variants (create → `occurrences`; add and date-move → `starts_at`, each landing on the right control); **the last-occurrence refusal** renders beside the remove control that provoked it (`loc ["path","occurrence_id"]` — the control is never pre-emptively disabled). **CK-17's error-affordance finding closed**: new shared `components/FieldError.tsx` (+ `FormLevelErrors`) renders every mapper message with `role="alert"`, an `aria-hidden` mark, and a fixed `.form-error` — the CK-17 finding was a CSS specificity bug (`.auth-screen p` outranked `.form-error`, so errors rendered grey at body size); `errorId`/`describedBy` moved into `lib/formErrors.ts` with an optional scope prefix for pages rendering one field key in several forms. **Optimistic-free**: every successful mutation re-fetches the detail (the server re-sorts by `starts_at` and stamps `updated_at`); every request follows the cancel-on-unmount convention (a `let cancelled` effect + an alive ref for handlers). Second minor backend finding reported: occurrence `location`/`map_url` are neither trimmed nor blank-rejected server-side, so `""` is storable. 30 frontend tests (10 new). Backend untouched, 114 green.
- **1.15.0** (2026-08-25): Phase CK-17 — gathering creation, list, and read-only detail (the frontend half of CK-16; **editing is CK-18**, deliberately — including the last-occurrence-refusal surface, which belongs with the delete control that provokes it). Three routes behind `RequireAuth`, linked from `/home`: **`/gatherings`** (kept gatherings newest first, each with the next-or-most-recent occurrence date; **the empty state is a real screen** with the create CTA — it is what every new account sees), **`/gatherings/new`** (type picker over the API's `GatheringType` values, title, decedent name rendered **when and only when** the type is `memorial` — a UX mirror of the backend gate, never a replacement: a blank decedent submits and the backend's 422 lands inline; multiple occurrence rows so a season is creatable whole), **`/gatherings/:id`** (read-only; **404 and non-permitted render one identical not-found screen** — the backend's 404-not-403 posture is only as good as the UI that renders it). **The timezone rule** (`decisions/2026-08-25-occurrence-time-entry.md`): a wall clock typed into the form is interpreted in the person's **profile** IANA zone (browser zone only when the profile has none), converted in ONE place — **`lib/datetime.ts`** — and the same zone renders every time back; the zone is stated visibly next to the time fields ("Times in America/Chicago"). Never `new Date(wallClock)` — that is the browser's zone: right on the dev machine, right in CI, silently wrong for anyone whose device and profile zones differ. **`lib/formErrors.ts` is the one 422→inline-error code path** for this and every future form: field errors land on the field their `loc` names (source segment dropped, dot-joined — `occurrences.0.starts_at`), errors with no visible field surface as form-level messages (**never swallowed**), the season-cap message renders verbatim (the backend owns the span arithmetic), and non-422 failures get a distinct non-validation message. `GET /gatherings` carries no occurrence data, so the list makes per-gathering detail fetches — **reported as a backend gap, not fixed here** (no backend source changed). 20 frontend tests (18 new: `lib/datetime.test.ts` pins conversion both directions against a zone forced to differ from the runner's; `lib/formErrors.test.ts`; `routes/gatherings.test.tsx` — decedent toggle, 422 landing on its field, unmappable 422 still rendering, empty state, detail 404). Backend untouched, 114 green.
- **1.14.0** (2026-08-25): Phase CK-16 — gathering and occurrence CRUD, the keeper schema's first surface (**backend only** — the frontend is CK-17, the CK-5/CK-6 split again). Migration 0010 is **one column**: `gatherings.updated_at` (the CK-7 `people.updated_at` precedent — the stamp arrives with the phase that makes the row mutable). New router **`app/api/gatherings.py`** (seven endpoints, standard auth gate): `POST`/`GET /gatherings`, `GET`/`PATCH /gatherings/{id}`, `POST /gatherings/{id}/occurrences`, `PATCH`/`DELETE /occurrences/{id}`. **Creation is ONE transaction and the ordering is the point**: gathering + occurrences + `keeping.keep()` commit together — the creator is first keeper and admin at once (keeper record §9.2), and a gathering never exists with zero keepers, even transiently within the request. **`requires_approval` is set `True` explicitly in application code on every create** (fail closed — no inviting context exists until the invitation phase): the column's `server_default=false` from 0008 is deliberately unchanged and never relied on; pinned by test, which works *because* the default is false — a `True` can only come from app code (database-schema decision 22). The gathering itself is created `live` — `requires_approval` moderates contributions *within* it, never its own visibility. Authorization is uniform and keeper-shaped: mutations require `admin_account_id`, reads require kept-or-admin — written against the keeper/admin facts, never "is creator" (the audience widens later); non-permitted access is **404, never 403**, byte-identical to a missing id. The memorial gate is validated in the Pydantic layer (field-level 422s, both directions — the CHECK stays the backstop, never the UX); the **season cap** (occurrences within one year of the earliest `starts_at`) is enforced app-layer at create, occurrence-add, **and date-move** (the kickoff named the first two; a patch that moves a date is the third way the span grows, so the cap holds there too); deleting the last occurrence is refused (a gathering with no dates is not a state this product has). The CK-13 deletion lapse now has a real API-created subject in test. `verify_schema.py` → head `0010`, 44 assertions (negative-tested via a temporary downgrade). 114 tests (16 new, `test_gatherings.py`); **conftest's per-test truncate extended to `accounts CASCADE`** — the first content-creating endpoints leave keeper-spine rows (gatherings, occurrences, kept_gatherings) that the people-only truncate never touched.
- **1.13.0** (2026-08-25): Phase CK-14 — brand modules and the one-word flip to **`CoveyKeep`** (`decisions/2026-08-20-name-gate.md` §6 settles the name, pending counsel; §4's centralization hedge is now closed — a rename is one file per side plus image assets). **`frontend/src/brand.ts`** (`PRODUCT_NAME`, `TAGLINE` — named exports, so lint catches an unused one) and **`backend/app/brand.py`** (`PRODUCT_NAME`, `FROM_DISPLAY_NAME`) are **the only homes for user-visible brand strings; two modules is deliberate** — the sides share no build, and a generated cross-language artefact would cost more than it saves at two constants; do not "fix" the duplication. Every user-visible instance now references a constant: sign-in heading + tagline, ToS placeholder copy, `index.html` title (a `%PRODUCT_NAME%` placeholder filled at build time by the `brandTitle` plugin in `vite.config.ts` — `order: 'pre'`, ahead of Vite's own env replacement), PWA manifest `name`/`short_name` (imported from brand.ts), FastAPI title, WebAuthn RP display name (display-only; the credential binds to the RP **ID**), magic-link + email-change subjects and bodies, and the provider From header (display name from brand — `EMAIL_FROM` stays the bare address; delivery is still console mode, so nothing was live). **The slug stays `covey-keep` everywhere** — repo, sites, services, database, paths, storage keys: filesystem convention, not branding. Pinned by test on both sides: `tests/test_brand.py` (3 new; a monkeypatched sentinel proves the subject is *built from* the constant, not a literal that happens to match) and `src/brand.test.tsx` under new frontend test tooling (vitest + jsdom + testing-library, `npm run test`). 98 backend tests; `theme_color` deliberately untouched (still the plugin default — theming phase).
- **1.12.0** (2026-08-25): Phase CK-13.1 — **`backend/scripts/verify_schema.py`**, a committed read-only verifier for a deployed migration's *resulting shape* (a green deploy proves only that `alembic upgrade head` ran). Read-only by construction **and enforced**: every statement a SELECT, plus `default_transaction_read_only=on` on the connection; exits non-zero on any failure; prints counts and shapes only — **never a personal-data column, never the connection string**. 42 assertions covering the 0008/0009 shape plus the **accounts-backfill integrity that only has real rows to check on Render** (the local DB is empty — that path is the one local tests never exercise). Targets `VERIFY_DATABASE_URL` if set, else the app's `DATABASE_URL`, reusing `config.py`'s asyncpg normalisation. **The external-URL TLS trap** is handled in the script and worth knowing: Render's *internal* URL is unencrypted inside their network, the *external* one requires TLS in libpq form, and **asyncpg has no `sslmode` parameter** — SQLAlchemy forwards leftover URL query parameters to the driver as kwargs, so an unstripped external URL dies with an unexpected-keyword `TypeError` that never mentions SSL. **Extending the assertions is part of shipping a migration** (`EXPECTED_REVISION` is a constant in the file); a failed assertion is a **finding, not a fix** — never relaxed to make it pass. No application code.
- **1.11.0** (2026-08-24): Phase CK-13 — migration 0009, the **keeper lifecycle half** (keeper record §2.3–2.7/§8/§9; completes CK-12's structural half). **`kept_gatherings`** (UNIQUE(account_id, gathering_id)) is **the single source of truth for both the reference count and the quota arithmetic** — no is-kept boolean, no counter column, anywhere. Grace is ONE timestamp: `gatherings.last_keeper_left_at`, stamped when the last keeper leaves, cleared when anyone keeps again; archive-at-30d/delete-at-90d are **derived** by `services/keeping.py::grace_state` from policy constants, never stored. **Memorials are exempt by type, not flag**: never stamped, excluded from `account_usage`, gated by the `memorial_decedent_name` CHECK (present iff type is `memorial` — the abuse gate). `gatherings.account_id` → **`created_by_account_id`** (immutable, historical — "owner" is not a concept) plus **`admin_account_id` NULL** (nullable IS the claimable state; unkeep relinquishes it); `groups.steward_person_id`/`backup_steward_person_id` → `admin_person_id`/`backup_admin_person_id`. `gatherings.total_bytes` (a maintained per-gathering fact — quota stays computed at request time by summing it over kept rows; the never-stored rule governs entitlement/quota, not this input). **Deletion lapses kept statuses** — the fourth deletion-path integration (CK-9 email-change requests, CK-10 WebAuthn, now kept rows): hard-delete, relinquish admin, stamp grace exactly as unkeep would. Service layer only — no endpoints, no frontend. 95 tests (13 new: `test_keeping.py` + a deletion-lapse test).
- **1.10.0** (2026-08-24): Phase CK-12 — migration 0008, the **keeper spine, structural half** (`decisions/2026-08-24-keeper-storage-model.md` §9; the lifecycle half — kept relation, refcounts, grace, memorial exemption, admin claim — is CK-13). **`accounts`** supertype (`PERSON|ORGANIZATION`): `people.account_id`/`organizations.account_id` UNIQUE NOT NULL point **at** it — accounts.id is the one FK target quota/subscription/keeping will reference; `/auth/verify` creates the account with the person, same transaction (still the one person-creation path). **`gatherings`** (typed — the event types + `season`/`memorial`/`church_gathering`; account-owned; `requires_approval` lives on the gathering, not the group profile) + **`occurrences`** (dates — RSVP/attendance/items hang per-occurrence; posts/media hang on the gathering with a nullable occurrence label) + **`gathering_invitations`** (the ONLY gathering↔group link; exactly-one-of group/sub-group/person). `events`/`event_series`/`event_type` **dropped**; written as a restructure, not a data migration — the tables were verified empty, and the NOT-NULL adds fail loudly otherwise. 82 tests (14 new in `test_schema_keeper.py`).
- **1.9.0** (2026-08-21): Phase CK-10 — migration 0007 (`webauthn_credentials`, `webauthn_challenges` — single-use 5-minute challenges, same discipline as every token), passkey enrolment (`/me/passkeys/*`, attestation `none`) and **usernameless** sign-in (`/auth/passkey/begin|complete` — empty `allowCredentials`, **no email field accepted in any form**: narrowing by address is the CK-5/CK-9 enumeration oracle again). Sign-in mints its session through `mint_session` in `auth.py` — **the one session-birth path**, shared with `/auth/verify`. py_webauthn + @simplewebauthn/browser are the deliberate library exception (crypto protocol surface, never hand-rolled). Deletion purges both new tables. **Passkeys are the security path, never presented as the easy one; a passkey is bound to its RP ID (`WEBAUTHN_RP_ID`) and dies with a domain change — UI stays unpromoted until the custom domain is final.**
- **1.8.0** (2026-08-20): Phase CK-9 — migration 0006 (`email_change_requests`, hashed-token conventions mirrored from `magic_link_tokens`), `POST`/`DELETE /me/email-change` + unauthenticated `GET /auth/email-change/verify` (**email is a credential, changed only by proof of control over the new inbox** — byte-identical 202 whether or not the address is taken, same enumeration rule as `/auth/request-link`); verification revokes every session but the requesting one; deletion purges `email_change_requests`. Sessions now **90-day sliding** (refresh at most daily, 365-day absolute cap from `issued_at`; JWT `exp` minted at the cap — the sessions row is the live authority); `/settings` email section + `/email-change` result screen.
- **1.7.0** (2026-08-20): Phase CK-8.1 — service-worker registration owned by `PwaUpdatePrompt` (`virtual:pwa-register/react`; update notice instead of any forced reload; hourly + tab-visible `registration.update()`); `injectRegister: null` in `vite.config.ts` **must stay `null`** — removing it registers the worker twice, and `false` silently strips `skipWaiting`/`clientsClaim` from the generated worker (loose `== null` check in vite-plugin-pwa 1.3.0).
- **1.6.0** (2026-08-20): Phase CK-8 — migration 0005 (`people.anonymized_at` — the stamp alone marks anonymization, no boolean), `POST /me/delete` (typed `DELETE` confirmation; anonymize + hard-delete auth material in one transaction; **deletion is anonymization, never cascade** — contributions and `tos_acceptances` retained), `get_auth_context` 401s anonymized people even on a valid JWT, `/settings` destructive section + `/account-deleted` screen.
- **1.5.0** (2026-08-20): Phase CK-7 — migration 0004 (`people.timezone` **IANA zone name, never a UTC offset**; `people.updated_at`; display-name CHECK), `/me/profile` GET+PATCH, `/settings` screen, silent one-time browser-timezone capture on first sign-in; `tzdata` in requirements (Windows has no system tzdb).
- **1.4.0** (2026-08-20): Phase CK-6 — frontend auth (sign-in screen, `/auth/callback` fragment handoff, localStorage session, react-router-dom); `/auth/verify` now 302s into the frontend; request-link body carries affirmative ToS consent; migration 0003.
- **1.3.0** (2026-08-19): Phase CK-5 — auth endpoints + migration 0002, pytest suite (requirements-dev.txt, test DB on the docker container), auth env surface (SESSION_SECRET et al.), PWA autoUpdate.
- **1.2.0** (2026-08-19): Phase CK-4 — Netlify + Render dev deploys (netlify.toml, render.yaml), CORS via ALLOWED_ORIGINS, frontend /health probe.
- **1.1.0** (2026-08-18): Phase CK-3 — dev DB via docker compose (host port 5434), SQLAlchemy models, Alembic migration 0001.
- **1.0.0** (2026-08-17): Initial creation at repo scaffold (Phase CK-2).
